{"parentUuid":"99661872-348d-4466-a018-bc9258f189ed","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- crucible: How to operate CRUCIBLE — the authorized, prove-don't-guess offensive-security engine in framework/v2. Read this BEFORE running an engagement/scan, re-verifying a finding, inspecting the event spine, reasoning about the veracity/anti-hallucination layer, the learning/metacognition core, or the safety stack. Use it when a task names CRUCIBLE, OBSIDIAN, `python3 -m framework.v2 <subcommand>`, findings/oracles/evidence certificates, the blackboard event spine, critics, reflection, or the reward/calibration loop.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. BEFORE invoking, check if the question is specific enough to research directly — if underspecified (e.g., \"what car to buy\" without budget/use-case/region), ask 2-3 clarifying questions to narrow scope. Then pass the refined question as args, weaving the answers in.\n- adversarial-sweep: Independent multi-lens adversarial re-attack of a defensive-oracle domain — the FORGE stage-9 companion to RED-PEN. Attack from disjoint lenses, refute each objection with a real repro, report only what survives. - Before merging any AEGIS/FORGE domain stream, alongside (not instead of) the red-pen agent. Pass the domain spec via args. On the email-auth stream it caught 3 CRITICAL/LOW defects PAST a RED-PEN PASS — near-zero-FP cannot be self-certified by one reviewer.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). LAZY import — nothing under aegis/ is imported until this\n131\t    # subcommand actually runs, so the scan/engage/benchmark gate path never touches it and\n132\t    # `make gate` stays byte-identical.\n133\t    from .aegis import cli as aegis_cli\n134\t    return aegis_cli.main(argv)\n135\t\n136\t\n137\tdef _evidence(argv: list[str]) -> int:\n138\t    from .evidence import cli as evidence_cli\n139\t    return evidence_cli.main(argv)\n140\t\n141\t\n142\tdef _mcp(argv: list[str]) -> int:\n143\t    from .mcp import cli as mcp_cli\n144\t    return mcp_cli.main(argv)\n145\t\n146\t\n147\tdef _report(argv: list[str]) -> int:\n148\t    from .report import cli as report_cli\n149\t    return report_cli.main(argv)\n150\t\n151\t\n152\tdef _status(argv: list[str]) -> int:\n153\t    \"\"\"One-shot environment summary: which backends are reachable, which\n154\t    paths resolve, which optional deps are installed.\"\"\"\n155\t    from .common import paths\n156\t    from .kernel import backends as backends_pkg\n157\t\n158\t    print(\"CRUCIBLE v2 status\")\n159\t    print(\"------------------\")\n160\t    try:\n161\t        root = paths.crucible_root()\n162\t        print(f\"  CRUCIBLE_ROOT     : {root}\")\n163\t    except CrucibleError as e:\n164\t        print(f\"  CRUCIBLE_ROOT     : ERROR — {e}\")\n165\t        return 2\n166\t\n167\t    print(f\"  v2 root           : {paths.v2_root()}\")\n168\t    print(f\"  memory db         : {paths.memory_db()}\")\n169\t    print(f\"  dryrun dir        : {paths.dryrun_dir()}\")\n170\t    print()\n171\t    print(\"  LLM backends      :\")\n172\t    for name, available, note in backends_pkg.probe_all():\n173\t        mark = \"✓\" if available else \"·\"\n174\t        print(f\"    {mark} {name:<10s} {note}\")\n175\t    print()\n176\t\n177\t    # X6 — surface the runtime governance state PROMINENTLY (not just a log line), so an operator\n178\t    # sees at a glance whether the sovereignty tier is sealed and whether capability entitlement\n179\t    # is actually enforced or the deployment is running UNGOVERNED.\n180\t    print(\"  Governance        :\")\n181\t    try:\n182\t        from .kernel import sovereignty\n183\t        pol = sovereignty.current()\n184\t        sealed = \" [SEALED]\" if sovereignty.is_sealed() else \" (unsealed — env-mutable)\"\n185\t        print(f\"    sovereignty tier : {pol.tier.name}{sealed}\")\n186\t    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — status must never crash on one subsystem\n187\t        print(f\"    sovereignty tier : ERROR — {type(e).__name__}: {e}\")\n188\t    try:\n189\t        from .entitlement.policy import EntitlementPolicy\n190\t        ent = EntitlementPolicy.from_provisioned()\n191\t        if ent.enforced:\n192\t            tier = ent.granted_tier.name if ent.granted_tier else \"—\"\n193\t            print(f\"    entitlement      : ENFORCED (granted tier {tier})\")\n194\t        else:\n195\t            print(f\"    entitlement      : ⚠ UNGOVERNED — {ent.explain()}\")\n196\t            print(\"                       exploitation runs unentitled; provision a trust root and set\")\n197\t            print(\"                       CRUCIBLE_ENTITLEMENT_ENFORCED to govern it.\")\n198\t    except Exception as e:  # Each `ChainEntry` hashes\n6\t`(seq, prev_hash, cert_digest)`, linking to its predecessor, so removing or reordering\n7\tany certificate breaks the chain at recomputation. A `SignedChainHead` — the last\n8\tentry's hash, its `last_seq`, and the entry count, signed by the governance trust root —\n9\tanchors the whole log; rewriting history requires forging a governance signature, and a\n10\tshrunk/back-dated head is caught by the monotonic `last_seq` anti-rollback check (the\n11\tsame defence the revocation high-water mark uses).\n12\t\n13\tDeterministic and append-only. Signing the head is provisioning; the runtime verifies.\n14\t\"\"\"\n15\t\n16\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n17\t\n18\tfrom ..entitlement.crypto import sign, verify_threshold\n19\tfrom ..entitlement.models import Signature, TrustRoot\n20\tfrom .canonical import canonical_json, evidence_signing_bytes, sha256_hex\n21\tfrom .models import _GENESIS_PREV, ChainEntry, SignedChainHead\n22\t\n23\t\n24\tdef _entry_hash(seq: int, prev_hash: str, cert_digest: str) -> str:\n25\t    return sha256_hex(canonical_json(\n26\t        {\"cert_digest\": cert_digest, \"prev_hash\": prev_hash, \"seq\": seq}))\n27\t\n28\t\n29\tdef build_chain(cert_digests: list[str], *, start_seq: int = 0) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n30\t    \"\"\"Build the hash chain over an ordered list of certificate digests.\"\"\"\n31\t    entries: list[ChainEntry] = []\n32\t    prev = _GENESIS_PREV\n33\t    for i, cd in enumerate(cert_digests):\n34\t        seq = start_seq + i\n35\t        eh = _entry_hash(seq, prev, cd)\n36\t        entries.append(ChainEntry(seq=seq, prev_hash=prev, cert_digest=cd, entry_hash=eh))\n37\t        prev = eh\n38\t    return entries\n39\t\n40\t\n41\tdef append_entry(entries: list[ChainEntry], cert_digest: str) -> ChainEntry:\n42\t    \"\"\"The next link after ``entries`` for ``cert_digest`` (append-only).\"\"\"\n43\t    prev = entries[-1].entry_hash if entries else _GENESIS_PREV\n44\t    seq = (entries[-1].seq + 1) if entries else 0\n45\t    return ChainEntry(seq=seq, prev_hash=prev, cert_digest=cert_digest,\n46\t                      entry_hash=_entry_hash(seq, prev, cert_digest))\n47\t\n48\t\n49\tdef verify_chain(entries: list[ChainEntry]) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n50\t    \"\"\"Recompute every link and the seq continuity. False (with the break location) if\n51\t    any entry was altered, deleted, or reordered.\"\"\"\n52\t    prev = _GENESIS_PREV\n53\t    for i, e in enumerate(entries):\n54\t        if e.prev_hash != prev:\n55\t            return False, f\"chain break at seq {e.seq}: prev_hash mismatch (entry deleted/reordered)\"\n56\t        if e.entry_hash != _entry_hash(e.seq, e.prev_hash, e.cert_digest):\n57\t            return False, f\"chain break at seq {e.seq}: entry_hash mismatch (entry tampered)\"\n58\t        if i > 0 and e.seq != entries[i - 1].seq + 1:\n59\t            return False, f\"chain break: seq gap at {e.seq}\"\n60\t        prev = e.entry_hash\n61\t    return True, f\"{len(entries)} entr{'y' if len(entries) == 1 else 'ies'} link cleanly\"\n62\t\n63\t\n64\tdef _head_payload(head: SignedChainHead) -> dict:\n65\t    # everything the head asserts EXCEPT its own signatures\n66\t    d = head.model_dump(mode=\"json\")\n67\t    d.pop(\"signatures\", None)\n68\t    return d\n69\t\n70\t\n71\tdef sign_head(entries: list[ChainEntry], *, engagement_slug: str,\n72\t              signers: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> SignedChainHead:\n73\t    \"\"\"Anchor the chain with a governance-signed head (PROVISIONING ONLY).\"\"\"\n74\t    head_hash = entries[-1].entry_hash if entries else _GENESIS_PREV\n75\t    last_seq = entries[-1].seq if entries else 0\n76\t    head = SignedChainHead(engagement_slug=engagement_slug, last_seq=last_seq,\n77\t                           entry_count=len(entries), head_hash=head_hash)\n78\t    msg = evidence_signing_bytes(_head_payload(head))\n79\t    sigs = [Signature(key_id=kid, signature_b64=sign(priv, msg)) for kid, priv in signers]\n80\t    return head.model_copy(update={\"signatures\": sigs})\n81\t\n82\t\n83\tdef verify_head(\n84\t    head: SignedChainHead,\n85\t    entries: list[ChainEntry],\n86\t    trust_root: TrustRoot,\n87\t    *,\n88\t    prev_highwater: int | None = None,\n89\t) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n90\t    \"\"\"Verify the chain links, that the head matches the chain, that its signature meets\n91\t    the governance threshold, and that it does not roll back below ``prev_highwater``.\"\"\"\n92\t    ok_chain, reason = verify_chain(entries)\n93\t    if not ok_chain:\n94\t        return False, reason\n95\t\n96\t    exp_hash = entries[-1].entry_hash if entries else _GENESIS_PREV\n97\t    exp_seq = entries[-1].seq if entries else 0\n98\t    if head.head_hash != exp_hash or head.last_seq != exp_seq or head.entry_count != len(entries):\n99\t        return False, \"head does not match the chain (log truncated or head rewritten)\"\n100\t\n101\t    thr = verify_threshold(evidence_signing_bytes(_head_payload(head)), head.signatures, trust_root)\n102\t    if not thr.satisfied:\n103\t        return False, f\"head signature invalid: {thr.reason}\"\n104\t\n105\t    if prev_highwater is not None and head.last_seq < prev_highwater:\n106\t        return False, (f\"rollback rejected: head last_seq {head.last_seq} < \"\n107\t                       f\"accepted high-water {prev_highwater}\")\n108\t\n109\t    return True, f\"chain of {len(entries)} entr{'y' if len(entries)==1 else 'ies'} anchored by a valid signed head\"\n110\t"}]},"uuid":"3f078506-a4ae-4966-93b7-f6850139623c","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:09:52.712Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ee329360-8552-4ada-a960-81e287fbdede","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"a88b0cd3-315e-467b-8526-dc6305e61feb","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d1e39661-f405-4984-bff2-6dce9f596a9d","agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015KffUxeyMyA5ux3SxGkkeG","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"\n2\tevidence.canonical — deterministic canonical bytes + digests for evidence integrity.\n3\t\n4\tA signature is over BYTES, and a digest binds a certificate to the EXACT evidence it\n5\twas judged on. Both need one canonical form, identical for producer and verifier. We\n6\treuse the same discipline the entitlement/authority layers use (sorted-key compact\n7\tUTF-8 JSON with a domain-separation prefix), so an evidence signature can never be\n8\treplayed as an entitlement/authority/revocation signature and vice versa.\n9\t\n10\tDomain tag is versioned and load-bearing: changing it invalidates every existing\n11\tevidence signature, so it never changes without a schema bump + migration.\n12\t\"\"\"\n13\t\n14\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n15\t\n16\timport hashlib\n17\timport json\n18\tfrom typing import Any, Final\n19\t\n20\t# Never change without a schema_version bump + migration (invalidates all evidence sigs).\n21\t_EVIDENCE_DOMAIN: Final[bytes] = b\"crucible-evidence-v1\\x00\"\n22\t\n23\t\n24\tdef canonical_json(payload: Any) -> bytes:\n25\t    \"\"\"Deterministic UTF-8 JSON: sorted keys (recursive), compact separators. The one\n26\t    form producer and verifier agree on.\"\"\"\n27\t    return json.dumps(\n28\t        payload, sort_keys=True, separators=(\",\", \":\"), ensure_ascii=False,\n29\t    ).encode(\"utf-8\")\n30\t\n31\t\n32\tdef sha256_hex(data: bytes) -> str:\n33\t    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tdef digest_payload(payload: Any) -> str:\n37\t    \"\"\"sha256 of a JSON-serialisable payload in canonical form — binds a certificate to\n38\t    the exact bytes of (e.g.) an oracle_context.\"\"\"\n39\t    return sha256_hex(canonical_json(payload))\n40\t\n41\t\n42\tdef evidence_signing_bytes(certificate_payload: dict[str, Any]) -> bytes:\n43\t    \"\"\"The exact bytes an authoriser signs / a verifier checks for an evidence\n44\t    certificate (the certificate's canonical JSON under the evidence domain tag).\"\"\"\n45\t    return _EVIDENCE_DOMAIN + canonical_json(certificate_payload)\n46\t"}]},"uuid":"8264ed28-ffd8-497b-8e62-e926519a6a3c","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:09:52.908Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a88b0cd3-315e-467b-8526-dc6305e61feb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

the chain must fail closed rather than\n32\t    anchor/verify a truncated log.\"\"\"\n33\t\n34\t\n35\t_PAGE = 5000   # replay page size for paging the full log to exhaustion\n36\t\n37\t\n38\tdef canonical-bytes discipline. ``engagement_id`` is\n41\t    bound in so two engagements with identical event content produce DIFFERENT chains — a\n42\t    signed head cannot be replayed onto a look-alike log (defense in depth alongside the\n43\t    slug check in ``verify_spine_head``).\"\"\"\n44\t    return digest_payload({\n45\t        \"engagement_id\": row.engagement_id,\n46\t        \"kind\": row.kind,\n47\t        \"agent_name\": row.agent_name,\n48\t        \"payload\": row.payload,\n49\t        \"parent_id\": row.parent_id,\n50\t        \"supersedes_id\": row.supersedes_id,\n51\t    })\n52\t\n53\t\n54\tdef _events(bb: Blackboard, engagement: str | int) -> list[BlackboardEventRow]:\n55\t    \"\"\"The FULL immutable history for an engagement in id (logical-clock) order — including\n56\t    superseded rows, because they are part of the append-only log and the chain covers them.\n57\t\n58\t    Pages ``replay`` to exhaustion (its default limit would silently truncate a large log,\n59\t    leaving the tail unanchored) and FAILS CLOSED: if the fetched count does not equal the\n60\t    live event count, it raises rather than anchor/verify a partial log.\"\"\"\n61\t    out: list[BlackboardEventRow] = []\n62\t    since = 0\n63\t    while True:\n64\t        batch = bb.replay(engagement=engagement, since_id=since,\n65\t {total} events — refusing to \"\n76\t            f\"anchor/verify a truncated log\")\n77\t    return out\n78\t\n79\t\n80\tdef build_spine_chain(bb: Blackboard, engagement: str | int) -> list[ChainEntry]:\n81\t    \"\"\"Build the hash-linked chain over the engagement's events, in id order.\"\"\"\n82\t    return build_chain([event_digest(r) for r in _events(bb, engagement)])\n83\t\n84\t\n85\tdef verify_spine_chain(bb: Blackboard, engagement: str | int,\n86\t                       entries: list[ChainEntry]) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n87\t    \"\"\"Verify ``entries`` against the LIVE events: the digests must equal the current events'\n88\t    digests in order (nothing altered/reordered/deleted) AND the links must recompute. Fails\n89\t    closed if the log cannot be read in full.\"\"\"\n90\t    try:\n91\t        live = [event_digest(r) for r in _events(bb, engagement)]\n92\t    except SpineChainError as e:\n93\t        return (False, f\"{e} — failing closed\")\n94\t    chain = [e.cert_digest for e in entries]\n95\t    if live != chain:\n96\t        return (False, f\"spine event-set mismatch: {len(chain)} chained vs {len(live)} live \"\n97\t                       f\"events — an event was altered, reordered, or deleted\")\n98\t    return verify_chain(entries)\n99\t\n100\t\n101\tdef sign_spine_head(bb: Blackboard, engagement: str, *,\n102\t                    signers: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> SignedChainHead:\n103\t    \"\"\"Anchor the spine with a governance-signed head over the current event log\n104\t    (PROVISIONING ONLY — the runtime only verifies).\"\"\"\n105\t    entries = build_spine_chain(bb, engagement)\n106\t    return sign_head(entries, engagement_slug=engagement, signers=signers)\n107\t\n108\t\n109\tdef verify_spine_head(bb: Blackboard, engagement: str, head: SignedChainHead,\n110\t                      trust_root, *, prev_highwater: int | None = None) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n111\t    \"\"\"Verify a signed spine head against the LIVE events: the head must be FOR this\n112\t    engagement (slug binding — no cross-engagement head replay), then the chain is rebuilt\n113\t    from the current log and checked against the signed head (hash + count), the signature\n114\t    meets the governance threshold, and the head has not rolled back. Any post-signing tamper\n115\t    — an edited payload, a reordered/deleted event, or an un-anchored appended event — fails\n116\t    here. Fails closed if the log cannot be read in full.\"\"\"\n117\t    if head.engagement_slug != engagement:\n118\t        return (False, f\"head is anchored to engagement {head.engagement_slug!r}, not \"\n119\t                       f\"{engagement!r} — refusing a cross-engagement head\")\n120\t    try:\n121\t        entries = build_spine_chain(bb, engagement)\n122\t    except SpineChainError as e:\n123\t        return (False, f\"{e} — failing closed\")\n124\t    return verify_head(head, entries, trust_root, prev_highwater=prev_highwater)\n125\t"}]},"uuid":"e4d7cc51-d698-479a-b796-df9a321a5253","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:15.112Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4fcf1cd4-249e-4bc7-9966-c36cc2906bc0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

The Python API below similarly does not expose\n14\tany mutating operation other than `post()` and the supersession\n15\thelper.\n16\t\"\"\"\n17\t\n18\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n19\t\n20\timport json\n21\timport sqlite3\n22\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n23\tfrom pathlib import Path\n24\tfrom typing import Any, Iterable\n25\t\n26\tfrom ..common import logging as v2log\n27\tfrom ..common import paths\n28\tfrom ..common.errors import CrucibleError\n29\tfrom .models import (\n30\t    ALL_EVENT_KINDS,\n31\t    BlackboardEvent,\n32\t    EventKind,\n33\t    PAYLOAD_BY_KIND,\n34\t    now_iso,\n35\t)\n36\t\n37\t\n38\t_log = v2log.get_logger(__name__)\n39\t\n40\t\n41\t_SCHEMA_SQL = Path(__file__).parent / \"schema.sql\"\n42\t\n43\t\n44\tdef blackboard_path() -> Path:\n45\t    return paths.v2_root() / \".blackboard\" / \"store.sqlite\"\n46\t\n47\t\n48\tclass BlackboardError(CrucibleError):\n49\t    \"\"\"Anything wrong with a blackboard operation (validation, IO).\"\"\"\n50\t\n51\t\n52\t@dataclass\n53\tclass BlackboardEventRow:\n54\t    \"\"\"Materialised view of a row, with payload already deserialised.\"\"\"\n55\t\n56\t    id: int\n57\t    engagement_id: int\n58\t    kind: EventKind\n59\t    agent_name: str\n60\t    posted_at: str\n61\t    payload: dict[str, Any]\n62\t    parent_id: int | None\n63\t    supersedes_id: int | None\n64\t\n65\t    @classmethod\n66\t    def from_sqlite(cls, row: sqlite3.Row) -> BlackboardEventRow:\n67\t        return cls(\n68\t            id=int(row[\"id\"]),\n69\t            engagement_id=int(row[\"engagement_id\"]),\n70\t            kind=row[\"kind\"],\n71\t            agent_name=row[\"agent_name\"],\n72\t            posted_at=row[\"posted_at\"],\n73\t            payload=json.loads(row[\"payload_json\"]),\n74\t            parent_id=row[\"parent_id\"],\n75\t            supersedes_id=row[\"supersedes_id\"],\n76\t        )\n77\t\n78\t\n79\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n80\t# Blackboard\n81\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n82\t\n83\t\n84\tclass Blackboard:\n85\t    \"\"\"Append-only event log for an engagement.\"\"\"\n86\t\n87\t    def __init__(self, *, db_path: Path | None = None) -> None:\n88\t        self.path = db_path or blackboard_path()\n89\t        paths.secure_dir(self.path.parent)            # owner-only spine dir (X2)\n90\t        self._conn = sqlite3.connect(self.path, detect_types=sqlite3.PARSE_DECLTYPES)\n91\t        paths.secure_existing(self.path)              # 0600 the spine DB (dir 0700 guards WAL sidecars)\n92\t        self._conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row\n93\t        self._conn.execute(\"PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON\")\n94\t        # X3: under WAL (set in schema.sql), synchronous=NORMAL drops the per-commit fsync\n95\t        # (durable across app crashes; only an OS/power crash can lose the last commit) — the\n96\t        # standard, safe WAL setting. The append-only log, its ids/posted_at and the immutability\n97\t        # triggers are unaffected; only the fsync per post() is elided.\n98\t        self._conn.execute(\"PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL\")\n99\t        self._migrate()\n100\t\n101\t    # context manager helpers\n102\t    def __enter__(self) -> Blackboard:\n103\t        return self\n104\t\n105\t    def __exit__(self, *exc: object) -> None:\n106\t        self.close()\n107\t\n108\t    def close(self) -> None:\n109\t        self._conn.close()\n110\t\n111\t    # ---- migrations ----\n112\t\n113\t    def _migrate(self) -> None:\n114\t        self._conn.executescript(_SCHEMA_SQL.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n115\t        self._conn.commit()\n116\t\n117\t    # ---- engagement registry ----\n118\t\n119\t    def engagement_id(self, slug: str, *, create: bool = True) -> int:\n120\t        row = self._conn.execute(\n121\t            \"SELECT id FROM bb_engagements WHERE slug = ?\", (slug,),\n122\t        ).fetchone()\n123\t        if row is not None:\n124\t            return int(row[\"id\"])\n125\t        if not create:\n126\t            raise BlackboardError(f\"no blackboard engagement with slug {slug!r}\")\n127\t        cur = self._conn.execute(\n128\t            \"INSERT INTO bb_engagements(slug, started_at) VALUES(?, ?)\",\n129\t            (slug, now_iso()),\n130\t        )\n131\t        self._conn.commit()\n132\t        eid = int(cur.lastrowid or 0)\n133\t        _log.info(\"agents.blackboard.engagement_started\", slug=slug, id=eid)\n134\t        return eid\n135\t\n136\t    def close_engagement(self, slug: str) -> None:\n137\t        self._conn.execute(\n138\t            \"UPDATE bb_engagements SET closed_at = ? WHERE slug = ?\",\n139\t            (now_iso(), slug),\n140\t        )\n141\t        self._conn.commit()\n142\t\n143\t    # ---- post (the only write path) ----\n144\t\n145\t    def post(\n146\t        self,\n147\t        *,\n148\t        engagement: str | int,\n149\t        kind: EventKind,\n150\t        agent_name: str,\n151\t        payload: dict[str, Any],\n152\t        parent_id: int | None = None,\n153\t        supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n154\t    ) -> int:\n155\t        \"\"\"Insert a new event. Validates payload against the kind's schema.\"\"\"\n156\t\n157\t        if kind not in ALL_EVENT_KINDS:\n158\t            raise BlackboardError(f\"unknown event kind {kind!r}\")\n159\t\n160\t        # Resolve engagement id\n161\t        eid = engagement if isinstance(engagement, int) else self.engagement_id(engagement)\n162\t\n163\t        # Validate payload\n164\t        payload_cls = PAYLOAD_BY_KIND[kind]\n165\t        try:\n166\t            validated = payload_cls.model_validate(payload).model_dump(by_alias=True)\n167\t        except Exception as e:\n168\t            raise BlackboardError(\n169\t                f\"payload for kind={kind!r} did not validate: {e}\"\n170\t            ) from e\n171\t\n172\t        # If supersedes_id is set, verify the target exists and matches kind/engagement.\n173\t        if supersedes_id is not None:\n174\t            row = self._conn.execute(\n175\t                \"SELECT engagement_id, kind FROM events WHERE id = ?\",\n176\t                (supersedes_id,),\n177\t            ).fetchone()\n178\t            if row is None:\n179\t                raise BlackboardError(\n180\t                    f\"supersedes_id={supersedes_id} not found in events\"\n181\t                )\n182\t            if int(row[\"engagement_id\"]) != eid:\n183\t                raise BlackboardError(\n184\t                    f\"supersedes_id={supersedes_id} belongs to a different engagement\"\n185\t                )\n186\t            if row[\"kind\"] != kind:\n187\t                raise BlackboardError(\n188\t                    f\"supersedes_id={supersedes_id} has kind={row['kind']!r}; \"\n189\t                    f\"new event has kind={kind!r}\"\n190\t                )\n191\t\n192\t        cur = self._conn.execute(\n193\t            \"\"\"\n194\t            INSERT INTO events\n195\t              (engagement_id, kind, agent_name, posted_at, payload_json,\n196\t               parent_id, supersedes_id)\n197\t            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n198\t            \"\"\",\n199\t            (eid, kind, agent_name, now_iso(),\n200\t             json.dumps(validated, default=str), parent_id, supersedes_id),\n201\t        )\n202\t        self._conn.commit()\n203\t        new_id = int(cur.lastrowid or 0)\n204\t        _log.info(\n205\t            \"agents.blackboard.posted\",\n206\t            event_id=new_id, engagement_id=eid, kind=kind, agent=agent_name,\n207\t            supersedes=supersedes_id,\n208\t        )\n209\t        return new_id\n210\t\n211\t    def supersede(\n212\t        self,\n213\t        *,\n214\t        old_id: int,\n215\t        new_payload: dict[str, Any],\n216\t        agent_name: str,\n217\t        parent_id: int | None = None,\n218\t    ) -> int:\n219\t        \"\"\"Convenience: post a new event with supersedes_id=old_id,\n220\t        copying engagement, kind, and (by default) parent_id from the\n221\t        old event so the provenance chain stays intact across edits.\n222\t\n223\t        Pass `parent_id` explicitly to override.\n224\t        \"\"\"\n225\t        row = self._conn.execute(\n226\t            \"SELECT engagement_id, kind, parent_id FROM events WHERE id = ?\",\n227\t            (old_id,),\n228\t        ).fetchone()\n229\t        if row is None:\n230\t            raise BlackboardError(f\"cannot supersede missing event id={old_id}\")\n231\t        if parent_id is None:\n232\t            parent_id = row[\"parent_id\"]\n233\t        return self.post(\n234\t            engagement=int(row[\"engagement_id\"]),\n235\t            kind=row[\"kind\"],\n236\t            agent_name=agent_name,\n237\t            payload=new_payload,\n238\t            parent_id=parent_id,\n239\t            supersedes_id=old_id,\n240\t        )\n241\t\n242\t    # ---- read ----\n243\t\n244\t    def get(self, event_id: int) -> BlackboardEventRow | None:\n245\t        row = self._conn.execute(\n246\t            \"SELECT * FROM events WHERE id = ?\", (event_id,),\n247\t        ).fetchone()\n248\t        return BlackboardEventRow.from_sqlite(row) if row is not None else None\n249\t\n250\t    def read(\n251\t        self,\n252\t        *,\n253\t        engagement: str | int,\n254\t        kinds: Iterable[EventKind] | None = None,\n255\t        agent: str | None = None,\n256\t        parent_id: int | None = None,\n257\t        since_id: int = 0,\n258\t        include_superseded: bool = False,\n259\t        limit: int = 1000,\n260\t    ) -> list[BlackboardEventRow]:\n261\t        \"\"\"Read events for an engagement. Defaults exclude superseded rows.\n262\t\n263\t        ``parent_id`` (X3) restricts to the direct children of one event — served by the\n264\t        ``idx_events_parent`` index, so a consumer that wants only the events posted ABOUT a\n265\t        given event (e.g. the critic verdicts on a finding) reads O(children) rows instead of\n266\t        scanning every event of that kind and filtering in Python. Additive: ``None`` (default)\n267\t        yields the identical query as before.\"\"\"\n268\t        eid = engagement if isinstance(engagement, int) else self.engagement_id(engagement, create=False)\n269\t\n270\t        sql = [\"SELECT e.* FROM events e\"]\n271\t        params: list[Any] = []\n272\t        if not include_superseded:\n273\t            sql.append(\n274\t                \"LEFT JOIN events s ON s.supersedes_id = e.id\"\n275\t            )\n276\t        sql.append(\"WHERE e.engagement_id = ?\")\n277\t        params.append(eid)\n278\t        if not include_superseded:\n279\t            sql.append(\"AND s.id IS NULL\")\n280\t        if parent_id is not None:\n281\t            sql.append(\"AND e.parent_id = ?\")\n282\t            params.append(parent_id)\n283\t        if since_id > 0:\n284\t            sql.append(\"AND e.id > ?\")\n285\t            params.append(since_id)\n286\t        if kinds:\n287\t            placeholders = \",\".join(\"?\" * len(list(kinds)))\n288\t            sql.append(f\"AND e.kind IN ({placeholders})\")\n289\t            params.extend(list(kinds))\n290\t        if agent is not None:\n291\t            sql.append(\"AND e.agent_name = ?\")\n292\t            params.append(agent)\n293\t        sql.append(\"ORDER BY e.id ASC LIMIT ?\")\n294\t        params.append(limit)\n295\t\n296\t        rows = self._conn.execute(\" \".join(sql), tuple(params)).fetchall()\n297\t        return [BlackboardEventRow.from_sqlite(r) for r in rows]\n298\t\n299\t    def replay(\n300\t        self,\n301\t        *,\n302\t        engagement: str | int,\n303\t        since_id: int = 0,\n304\t        kinds: Iterable[EventKind] | None = None,\n305\t        include_superseded: bool = False,\n306\t        limit: int = 100_000,\n307\t    ) -> list[BlackboardEventRow]:\n308\t        \"\"\"Replay the event spine in strict id (logical-clock) order from ``since_id`` — the\n309\t        unified subscribe/replay API every subsystem reads the stream through. A consumer polls\n310\t        with the last id it saw as ``since_id`` to receive only new events (a durable cursor);\n311\t        ``since_id=0`` replays the whole stream. Superseded rows are excluded by default so a\n312\t        replay reflects the current view — pass ``include_superseded=True`` for the full,\n313\t        never-edited history (the audit view). Read-only; the log stays append-only.\"\"\"\n314\t        return self.read(engagement=engagement, since_id=since_id, kinds=kinds,\n315\t                         include_superseded=include_superseded, limit=limit)\n316\t\n317\t    def count(\n318\t        self, *, engagement: str | int, kind: EventKind | None = None,\n319\t    ) -> int:\n320\t        eid = engagement if isinstance(engagement, int) else self.engagement_id(engagement, create=False)\n321\t        if kind is None:\n322\t            row = self._conn.execute(\n323\t                \"SELECT COUNT(*) AS c FROM events WHERE engagement_id = ?\", (eid,),\n324\t            ).fetchone()\n325\t        else:\n326\t            row = self._conn.execute(\n327\t                \"SELECT COUNT(*) AS c FROM events \"\n328\t                \"WHERE engagement_id = ? AND kind = ?\",\n329\t                (eid, kind),\n330\t            ).fetchone()\n331\t        return int(row[\"c\"])\n332\t\n333\t    def latest_event_id(self, *, engagement: str | int) -> int:\n334\t        eid = engagement if isinstance(engagement, int) else self.engagement_id(engagement, create=False)\n335\t        row = self._conn.execute(\n336\t            \"SELECT MAX(id) AS m FROM events WHERE engagement_id = ?\", (eid,),\n337\t        ).fetchone()\n338\t        return int(row[\"m\"] or 0)\n339\t\n340\t\n341\tdef open_blackboard(*, db_path: Path | None = None) -> Blackboard:\n342\t    return Blackboard(db_path=db_path)\n343\t"}]},"uuid":"7c921c2a-76f2-4c16-a8dd-b8e85cab2db7","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:15.631Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"02929d6c-f64a-423d-86cc-f2d9aff6f1c9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"364b4f4b-1e6a-4f56-bfbe-a4e81d764dc8","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d1e39661-f405-4984-bff2-6dce9f596a9d","agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0154Fzp5X51H75KvP1FHyVMu","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"\n2\tveracity.firewall — admit(): the one choke point every claim must cross.\n3\t\n4\t`admit()` turns a `Claim` into an `AdmittedClaim` by RE-EXECUTING each cited ground —\n5\tnever trusting a string — and labelling the result. It is the reusable generalization of\n6\tthe discipline the scanner already applies at promotion time, made universal:\n7\t\n8\t    contradiction?  → CONTRADICTED   (the world-model refutes an asserted entity)\n9\t    named entity not in the graph?   → UNGROUNDED   (a fabricated target)\n10\t    ORACLE token re-fires?           → GROUNDED (fact) via verify.reverify\n11\t    CERT token 4-layer verifies?     → GROUNDED (fact) via evidence.verify_certificate\n12\t    WORLDMODEL node belief-floored + provenance grounded? → GROUNDED (fact)\n13\t    HYPOTHESIS gated + prior≤cap?    → GROUNDED (hypothesis — labelled, never a fact)\n14\t    none of the above                → UNGROUNDED   (labelled commentary, never dropped)\n15\t\n16\tThe layer only ever DEMOTES or abstains: it can turn a fabricated \"confirmed\" into\n17\tUNGROUNDED, but it can NEVER promote a claim the oracle refused. A token whose backing\n18\tLLM call was a dry-run cannot ground. This is what makes hallucination structurally\n19\tunshippable rather than merely discouraged.\n20\t\n21\tA ground is BOUND to the claim it backs: an oracle must re-fire for the CLAIM's own\n22\tbug_class (a SQLi proof cannot ground an RCE claim), a cert must certify that bug_class,\n23\tand a world-model node must be one the claim names AND whose provenance traces to a real\n24\toracle/cert (an allowlist — collected intelligence and derivations do not reach fact\n25\tstrength). A fact claim must declare its subject; an unbound proof grounds nothing.\n26\t\n27\tNOTE ON PHASING: this is the caller-less PRIMITIVE (veracity P0). Runtime enforcement is\n28\twired in the subsequent phases — the world-model admission gate (P2) routes every\n29\tadd_node/add_edge through admit(), and the reporting gate (P4) binds report prose. Until\n30\tthen admit() is exercised only by its tests; that is by design, not a gap.\n31\t\"\"\"\n32\t\n33\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n34\t\n35\tfrom ..intel.predict import _PRIOR_CAP\n36\tfrom .claims import AdmittedClaim, Claim, VeracityVerdict\n37\tfrom .consistency import contradicts\n38\tfrom .tokens import Ground, GroundingToken\n39\t\n40\t_BELIEF_FLOOR = 0.5    # a world-model node grounds a fact only above this evidence-discounted belief\n41\t_LCB_Z = 1.0\n42\tdef _provenance_traces_to_proof(provenance: str) -> bool:\n43\t    # Single source of truth: a world-model node grounds a FACT only if its provenance\n44\t    # classifies as GROUNDED (traces to a fired oracle / signed cert / promoted finding).\n45\t    # Collected intelligence and derivations are legitimate but do NOT reach fact strength.\n46\t    from ..worldmodel.models import GROUNDING_GROUNDED, classify_provenance\n47\t    return classify_provenance(provenance) == GROUNDING_GROUNDED\n48\t\n49\t\n50\tdef _oracle_ok(tok: GroundingToken, claim: \"Claim\", verifier) -> tuple[bool, float]:\n51\t    \"\"\"The oracle grounds the claim only if it RE-FIRES for the CLAIM's OWN bug_class —\n52\t    a SQLi context cannot ground a 'balance drain' claim, because reverifying it under\n53\t    that bug_class won't fire. Returns (ok, re-executed confidence).\"\"\"\n54\t    if tok.from_dryrun or not isinstance(tok.oracle_context, dict) or not tok.oracle_context:\n55\t        return (False, 0.0)\n56\t    bug_class = claim.bug_class or tok.bug_class\n57\t    if claim.bug_class and tok.bug_class and claim.bug_class != tok.bug_class:\n58\t        return (False, 0.0)   # the token claims to prove a different subject than the claim\n59\t    from ..verify.reverify import reverify_context\n60\t    r = reverify_context(tok.oracle_context, bug_class=bug_class,\n61\t                         claimed_confirmed_by=tok.claimed_confirmed_by,\n62\t                         claimed_confidence=tok.claimed_confidence, verifier=verifier)\n63\t    return (r.ok, r.confidence)\n64\t\n65\t\n66\tdef _cert_ok(tok: GroundingToken, claim: \"Claim\", trust_root) -> bool:\n67\t    if tok.from_dryrun or trust_root is None or not isinstance(tok.signed_evidence, dict):\n68\t        return False\n69\t    from ..evidence.certify import verify_certificate\n70\t    from ..evidence.models import SignedEvidence\n71\t    try:\n72\t        signed = SignedEvidence.model_validate(tok.signed_evidence)\n73\t    except Exception:\n74\t        return False\n75\t    # bind: the certificate must certify the CLAIM's subject, not an unrelated finding.\n76\t    if claim.bug_class and signed.certificate.bug_class and signed.certificate.bug_class != claim.bug_class:\n77\t        return False\n78\t    return verify_certificate(signed, oracle_context=tok.oracle_context or {},\n79\t                              trust_root=trust_root).ok\n80\t\n81\t\n82\tdef _worldmodel_ok(tok: GroundingToken, claim: \"Claim\", world, belief_floor: float) -> bool:\n83\t    if world is None or not tok.node_id:\n84\t        return False\n85\t    # bind: the claim must actually be ABOUT this node (name it in entity_refs).\n86\t    if tok.node_id not in (claim.entity_refs or []):\n87\t        return False\n88\t    node = world.get_node(tok.node_id)\n89\t    if node is None:\n90\t        return False\n91\t    lcb = node.belief_lcb(_LCB_Z) if hasattr(node, \"belief_lcb\") else node.belief_mean\n92\t    # belief-floored AND its provenance traces to a real oracle/cert proof.\n93\t    return lcb >= belief_floor and _provenance_traces_to_proof(node.provenance)\n94\t\n95\t\n96\tdef _hypothesis_ok(tok: GroundingToken) -> bool:\n97\t    return (not tok.from_dryrun and tok.gated is True\n98\t            and tok.prior is not None and tok.prior <= _PRIOR_CAP)\n99\t\n100\t\n101\t# strongest → weakest FACT ground; HYPOTHESIS is grounded-but-not-fact\n102\t_FACT_ORDER = (Ground.ORACLE, Ground.CERT, Ground.WORLDMODEL)\n103\t\n104\t\n105\tdef admit(\n106\t    claim: Claim,\n107\t    *,\n108\t    world=None,\n109\t    trust_root=None,\n110\t    verifier=None,\n111\t    belief_floor: float = _BELIEF_FLOOR,\n112\t    require_entities: bool = True,\n113\t) -> AdmittedClaim:\n114\t    \"\"\"Admit a claim by re-executing its grounds. See the module docstring for the\n115\t    decision order. Never mutates; never promotes an oracle-refused claim.\"\"\"\n116\t    # 1. contradiction — the world-model actively refutes a named entity.\n117\t    contra, score, creason = contradicts(claim.entity_refs, world)\n118\t    if contra:\n119\t        return AdmittedClaim(claim=claim, verdict=VeracityVerdict.CONTRADICTED,\n120\t                             reason=creason)\n121\t\n122\t    # 2. fabricated entity — a named target that does not exist in the graph.\n123\t    if require_entities and world is not None and claim.entity_refs:\n124\t        missing = [r for r in claim.entity_refs if not world.has_node(r)]\n125\t        if missing:\n126\t            return AdmittedClaim(\n127\t                claim=claim, verdict=VeracityVerdict.UNGROUNDED,\n128\t                reason=f\"claim names entities absent from the world-model (fabricated?): {missing}\")\n129\t\n130\t    # 3. validate each ground by RE-EXECUTION, BOUND to this claim's subject; keep the\n131\t    #    strongest that resolves. A ground proves THIS claim, not merely that a proof\n132\t    #    exists somewhere — the oracle must re-fire for the claim's own bug_class, the\n133\t    #    cert must certify it, the world-model node must be one the claim names.\n134\t    resolved: set[Ground] = set()\n135\t    oracle_conf: float | None = None\n136\t    for tok in claim.tokens:\n137\t        if tok.ground is Ground.ORACLE:\n138\t            ok, conf = _oracle_ok(tok, claim, verifier)\n139\t            if ok:\n140\t                resolved.add(Ground.ORACLE)\n141\t                oracle_conf = conf if oracle_conf is None else max(oracle_conf, conf)\n142\t        elif tok.ground is Ground.CERT:\n143\t            if _cert_ok(tok, claim, trust_root):\n144\t                resolved.add(Ground.CERT)\n145\t        elif tok.ground is Ground.WORLDMODEL:\n146\t            if _worldmodel_ok(tok, claim, world, belief_floor):\n147\t                resolved.add(Ground.WORLDMODEL)\n148\t        elif tok.ground is Ground.HYPOTHESIS:\n149\t            if _hypothesis_ok(tok):\n150\t                resolved.add(Ground.HYPOTHESIS)\n151\t\n152\t    # 4. a dry-run claim can only stand on re-executable proof (oracle/cert), never on\n153\t    #    its own LLM reasoning (worldmodel-via-llm / hypothesis).\n154\t    if claim.from_dryrun:\n155\t        resolved &= {Ground.ORACLE, Ground.CERT}\n156\t\n157\t    # 5. label — fact strength first, then hypothesis, then ungrounded.\n158\t    fact = next((g for g in _FACT_ORDER if g in resolved), None)\n159\t    if fact is not None:\n160\t        # a FACT claim must declare a subject the ground is bound to: bug_class for\n161\t        # oracle/cert (the re-fire already checked it), a named entity for worldmodel\n162\t        # (the node-in-entity_refs binding already checked it).\n163\t        if fact in (Ground.ORACLE, Ground.CERT) and not claim.bug_class:\n164\t            return AdmittedClaim(\n165\t                claim=claim, verdict=VeracityVerdict.UNGROUNDED,\n166\t                reason=\"a re-executed proof is present but the claim declares no bug_class \"\n167\t                       \"subject to bind it to — an unbound proof grounds nothing\")\n168\t        # the calibrated confidence is the RE-EXECUTED oracle value, not a proposer number.\n169\t        conf = oracle_conf if (fact is Ground.ORACLE and oracle_conf is not None) else claim.proposed_confidence\n170\t        return AdmittedClaim(claim=claim, verdict=VeracityVerdict.GROUNDED, strength=fact,\n171\t                             grounded_by=[g.value for g in resolved], calibrated_confidence=conf,\n172\t                             reason=f\"grounded by re-executed {fact.value} bound to the claim's subject\")\n173\t    if Ground.HYPOTHESIS in resolved:\n174\t        prior = next((t.prior for t in claim.tokens if t.ground is Ground.HYPOTHESIS), None)\n175\t        return AdmittedClaim(claim=claim, verdict=VeracityVerdict.GROUNDED, strength=Ground.HYPOTHESIS,\n176\t                             grounded_by=[\"hypothesis\"], calibrated_confidence=prior,\n177\t                             reason=\"admitted as a gated, prior-capped hypothesis (not a fact)\")\n178\t    return AdmittedClaim(\n179\t        claim=claim, verdict=VeracityVerdict.UNGROUNDED,\n180\t        reason=\"no cited ground re-verified — rendered as labelled analyst commentary, never as fact\")\n181\t"}]},"uuid":"4a0aeb87-74a7-4a39-ae52-95146b30b3c1","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:16.070Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"364b4f4b-1e6a-4f56-bfbe-a4e81d764dc8","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"567bcb4c-1704-43b8-aa6b-94aac8a5f850","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d1e39661-f405-4984-bff2-6dce9f596a9d","agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01X4VBg5VbVqFePG6qXgwcAF","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"\n2\tagents.scope_gate — pre-flight validator for any HTTP action.\n3\t\n4\tWraps the inviolable gates from `common.ethics` and translates them\n5\tinto a structured `ScopeDecision` so the executor can post a\n6\tScopeViolation event on the blackboard rather than crashing the\n7\tcoordinator. The exceptions still exist; this module makes them\n8\tinspectable without hiding them.\n9\t\n10\tFive checks, in order:\n11\t\n12\t  1. Charter file exists.\n13\t  2. Charter has a non-placeholder operator signature.\n14\t  3. Action's URL is a valid http/https URL.\n15\t  4. URL's host is in the parsed charter scope.\n16\t  5. Posture-scoped extra restrictions (e.g. EMULATE refuses\n17\t     EMULATE-prohibited methods unless the charter authorises them).\n18\t\n19\tRefused actions never reach the wire. This module is the only\n20\tplace where charter / scope gating happens for HttpExecutor.\n21\t\"\"\"\n22\t\n23\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n24\t\n25\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n26\tfrom typing import Literal\n27\tfrom urllib.parse import urlparse\n28\t\n29\tfrom ..common import ethics\n30\tfrom ..common.errors import (\n31\t    CharterMissing,\n32\t    CharterNotSigned,\n33\t    OutOfScope,\n34\t)\n35\t\n36\t\n37\tPosture = Literal[\"TEST\", \"AUDIT\", \"EMULATE\"]\n38\tDESTRUCTIVE_METHODS = frozenset({\"POST\", \"PUT\", \"DELETE\", \"PATCH\"})\n39\t\n40\t# Path patterns that are destructive on most apps. URL contains any of\n41\t# these → executor must prompt the operator before issuing.\n42\t_DESTRUCTIVE_PATH_TOKENS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n43\t    \"/admin\", \"/delete\", \"/destroy\", \"/drop\", \"/wipe\",\n44\t    \"/upload\", \"/import\", \"/restore\",\n45\t    \"/reset\", \"/password-reset\", \"/forgot-password\",\n46\t    \"/withdraw\", \"/transfer\", \"/refund\", \"/payout\",\n47\t    \"/payment/\", \"/checkout\", \"/subscribe\", \"/unsubscribe\",\n48\t    \"/sudo\", \"/impersonate\", \"/grant\", \"/promote\",\n49\t)\n50\t\n51\t\n52\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n53\tclass ScopeDecision:\n54\t    \"\"\"Outcome of running an action through the scope gate.\n55\t\n56\t    `allowed=True` is necessary AND sufficient for the executor to\n57\t    proceed; `allowed=False` is mandatory grounds to refuse and post\n58\t    a ScopeViolation event. The reason is human-readable and the\n59\t    structured fields drive programmatic logging.\n60\t    \"\"\"\n61\t\n62\t    allowed: bool\n63\t    reason: str\n64\t    method: str = \"\"\n65\t    url: str = \"\"\n66\t    host: str = \"\"\n67\t    is_destructive: bool = False\n68\t    refusal_kind: Literal[\n69\t        \"\",\n70\t        \"charter_missing\",\n71\t        \"charter_unsigned\",\n72\t        \"url_invalid\",\n73\t        \"out_of_scope\",\n74\t        \"posture_forbidden\",\n75\t    ] = \"\"\n76\t\n77\t\n78\tdef is_destructive(method: str, url: str) -> bool:\n79\t    \"\"\"Heuristic destructive classifier. Erring toward refusal is the\n80\t    correct default — a false positive costs one operator prompt;\n81\t    a false negative could cost an engagement.\"\"\"\n82\t    if method.upper() in DESTRUCTIVE_METHODS:\n83\t        return True\n84\t    path = urlparse(url).path.lower()\n85\t    for tok in _DESTRUCTIVE_PATH_TOKENS:\n86\t        if tok in path:\n87\t            return True\n88\t    return False\n89\t\n90\t\n91\tdef parse_url(target_url: str) -> tuple[str, str]:\n92\t    \"\"\"Normalise into (scheme, host). Raises ValueError if the URL is\n93\t    not parseable as http/https. A bare IPv6 literal is bracketed first so its\n94\t    host is not truncated (``fe80::1`` would otherwise parse as host ``fe80``).\"\"\"\n95\t    if \"://\" not in target_url:\n96\t        target_url = \"https://\" + target_url\n97\t    parsed = urlparse(ethics.bracket_bare_ipv6(target_url))\n98\t    if parsed.scheme not in (\"http\", \"https\"):\n99\t        raise ValueError(f\"unsupported scheme: {parsed.scheme!r}\")\n100\t    if not parsed.hostname:\n101\t        raise ValueError(f\"no hostname in URL: {target_url!r}\")\n102\t    return parsed.scheme, parsed.hostname\n103\t\n104\t\n105\tdef validate_action(\n106\t    *,\n107\t    slug: str,\n108\t    method: str,\n109\t    target_url: str,\n110\t    posture: Posture = \"TEST\",\n111\t) -> ScopeDecision:\n112\t    \"\"\"Run the full pre-flight validation chain for one action.\n113\t\n114\t    The decision is the conjunction of all checks. The first failing\n115\t    check populates the refusal_kind; later checks are not evaluated.\n116\t    \"\"\"\n117\t    method_norm = method.upper().strip() or \"GET\"\n118\t    destructive = is_destructive(method_norm, target_url)\n119\t\n120\t    # 1+2. Charter file + signature.\n121\t    try:\n122\t        ethics.require_charter_signed(slug)\n123\t    except CharterMissing as exc:\n124\t        return ScopeDecision(\n125\t            allowed=False, reason=str(exc),\n126\t            method=method_norm, url=target_url,\n127\t            is_destructive=destructive,\n128\t            refusal_kind=\"charter_missing\",\n129\t        )\n130\t    except CharterNotSigned as exc:\n131\t        return ScopeDecision(\n132\t            allowed=False, reason=str(exc),\n133\t            method=method_norm, url=target_url,\n134\t            is_destructive=destructive,\n135\t            refusal_kind=\"charter_unsigned\",\n136\t        )\n137\t\n138\t    # 3. URL parsing.\n139\t    try:\n140\t        _scheme, host = parse_url(target_url)\n141\t    except ValueError as exc:\n142\t        return ScopeDecision(\n143\t            allowed=False, reason=f\"unparseable URL: {exc}\",\n144\t            method=method_norm, url=target_url,\n145\t            is_destructive=destructive,\n146\t            refusal_kind=\"url_invalid\",\n147\t        )\n148\t\n149\t    # 4. Scope check (uses ethics.require_in_scope under the hood).\n150\t    try:\n151\t        ethics.require_in_scope(slug, target_url)\n152\t    except OutOfScope as exc:\n153\t        return ScopeDecision(\n154\t            allowed=False, reason=str(exc),\n155\t            method=method_norm, url=target_url, host=host,\n156\t            is_destructive=destructive,\n157\t            refusal_kind=\"out_of_scope\",\n158\t        )\n159\t\n160\t    # 5. Posture-scoped restrictions. Currently EMULATE-prohibited\n161\t    # methods are NOT defined in v1 canon; this is a hook for future\n162\t    # tightening. TEST and AUDIT pass here.\n163\t    # (Intentionally no extra checks today — adding any would go\n164\t    #  beyond what opsec-discipline.md authorises.)\n165\t\n166\t    return ScopeDecision(\n167\t        allowed=True,\n168\t        reason=f\"in scope; charter signed; posture={posture}\",\n169\t        method=method_norm, url=target_url, host=host,\n170\t        is_destructive=destructive,\n171\t    )\n172\t"}]},"uuid":"924fc227-379c-4439-9d33-f9d511657a29","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:27.552Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"567bcb4c-1704-43b8-aa6b-94aac8a5f850","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"a62a9bbc-f626-4d62-8fb4-4698a9b6e78b","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011CdCRCzqg63XHcPHKrv9H6","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01UpRzvvAhBKkpZVa39GMmbu","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main/framework/v2/docs/CRUCIBLE-AI.md"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":6181,"cache_read_input_tokens":49490,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":6181,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdCRCyUpiwsJTaUkyPzva","attributionAgent":"Explore","type":"assistant","uuid":"d6be9790-379d-4e34-97ca-1884ba7ff80e","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:35.224Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
{"parentUuid":"d6be9790-379d-4e34-97ca-1884ba7ff80e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d1e39661-f405-4984-bff2-6dce9f596a9d","agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UpRzvvAhBKkpZVa39GMmbu","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# CRUCIBLE — the AI's guide to the system, its agents, and its spine\n2\t\n3\tThis is the reasoning agent's map of CRUCIBLE: what the system is, the agents that run it, the\n4\tone immutable event stream they share, the anti-hallucination layer that makes claims\n5\ttrustworthy, the learning/metacognition core that makes it improve honestly, and the safety\n6\tstack that keeps it inside its authorization. It is written to match the **shipped** code — where\n7\ta mechanism is a primitive not yet wired into a live loop, it says so. For the design rationale\n8\tsee [ARCHITECTURE.md](./ARCHITECTURE.md); for operating it see [OPERATOR-GUIDE.md](./OPERATOR-GUIDE.md);\n9\tthe binding constitution is [/CLAUDE.md](../../../CLAUDE.md); the runtime doctrine injected into\n10\tevery LLM call is [/framework/cognitive/metacognition.md](../../../framework/cognitive/metacognition.md).\n11\t\n12\t---\n13\t\n14\t## 0. The one rule\n15\t\n16\t> A finding is `confirmed` for exactly one reason: a **deterministic oracle fired** at or above\n17\t> threshold over data a **real target actually produced** (`verify/verifier.py`). The LLM\n18\t> proposes; the oracle disposes.\n19\t\n20\tEvery other subsystem is subordinate to this. Any AI addition — critics, reinforcement learning,\n21\treflection, self-consistency — may only **advise, re-rank, defer, or abstain**. None may promote\n22\ta claim the oracle refused, and none may silently skip an authorized attack surface.\n23\t\n24\t---\n25\t\n26\t## 1. Two execution paths, one spine\n27\t\n28\t- **Flagship engage/scan** (`engage.py`, `scanner/`): crawl → audit insertion points → confirm\n29\t  via oracles → chain attack paths → score confidence. Synchronous, best-effort at every\n30\t  value-add stage (a failure never sinks the run).\n31\t- **Multi-agent orchestration (MAO)** (`agents/coordinator.py` over `agents/blackboard.py`):\n32\t  specialist agents (recon, hypothesis, exploit, critique, reporter, memory) post typed events;\n33\t  the coordinator schedules them and refuses to quiesce while findings are unreviewed.\n34\t\n35\tHistorically these were separate worlds. The **event spine** unifies them: `engage` can mirror\n36\tits whole run onto the blackboard (`run_engagement(spine=…)` / `--spine`), so one immutable,\n37\treplayable stream carries the entire engagement.\n38\t\n39\t---\n40\t\n41\t## 2. The event spine (`agents/blackboard.py`)\n42\t\n43\tThe blackboard is an **append-only, typed, provenance-linked, seq-clocked** SQLite event log —\n44\tthe single stream every subsystem communicates through.\n45\t\n46\t- **Kinds** (`agents/models.py`, each with a validated payload): `observation`, `hypothesis`,\n47\t  `plan`, `action`, `result`, `finding`, `critique`, `decision`, and the nervous-system kinds\n48\t  `reward`, `critic_verdict`, `reflection`, `refusal`. A `critic_verdict` is `endorse|object|\n49\t  abstain` — it **cannot** say \"confirm\" (oracle authority at the type level).\n50\t- **Write path**: only `post()` (and `supersede()` — edits are new rows referencing the old).\n51\t  SQL triggers refuse UPDATE/DELETE.\n52\t- **Read/replay**: `read(...)` and `replay(engagement, since_id=…)` — a durable cursor a\n53\t  consumer polls for new events.\n54\t- **Bridge**: `agents/spine_sink.py::SpineSink` implements the scanner's `ProgressSink` Protocol\n55\t  (real-time phases/findings → observation events) plus typed helpers (`finding_event`,\n56\t  `refusal`, `reward`, `decision`, `reflection`, `critic_verdict`). Best-effort — a spine write\n57\t  can never perturb a run.\n58\t- **Cryptographic tamper-evidence**: `agents/spine_chain.py` hash-links the events and anchors\n59\t  them with a governance-signed head (reusing `evidence/chain.py`). `verify_spine_head` rebuilds\n60\t  the chain from the live log and fails on any altered/reordered/deleted/appended-after-signing\n61\t  event; it pages the full log and fails **closed** on a truncated read, and binds the head to\n62\t  its engagement (no cross-engagement replay). Purely additive; no schema change.\n63\t\n64\t---\n65\t\n66\t## 3. The anti-hallucination (veracity) layer — P0–P7\n67\t\n68\tThe one place grounding was enforced (scanner promotion) is now universal and unforgeable.\n69\t**No claim becomes a fact unless a cited grounding token RE-EXECUTES** — the oracle re-fires, a\n70\tsigned cert re-verifies, a belief traces to proof, or it is a gated, capped hypothesis. The\n71\tlayer only ever **demotes or abstains**.\n72\t\n73\t- **P0/P1 — `veracity/`**: `firewall.admit()` re-executes each cited ground *bound to the claim's\n74\t  subject* (a SQLi proof cannot ground an RCE claim). `critique_agent` reserves `confirmed` for a\n75\t  fired oracle; an LLM-only verdict is `llm_advisory`.\n76\t- **P2 — world-model admission**: every `worldmodel` write is tagged with a provenance grounding\n77\t  tier; belief is byte-identical by default.\n78\t- **P3 — live over findings**: `veracity/adapters.py::admit_finding` runs the firewall over real\n79\t  findings in the engage loop; a finding whose proof no longer reproduces is demoted.\n80\t- **P4 — reporting gate**: report sentences bound into the `EvidenceCertificate`\n81\t  (`evidence/`) so `verify_certificate`/`verify_bundle` fail closed when a report sentence has no\n82\t  backing evidence; the reporter re-executes each finding's oracle at report time; the scanner\n83\t  export states each finding's live grounding; attack paths become fail-closed, head-anchored\n84\t  path certificates. (A deterministic gate certifies the *structured* claim + tamper-evidence; it\n85\t  does **not** do natural-language entailment — that boundary is stated honestly.)\n86\t- **P5 — kernel self-consistency** (`kernel/consistency.py`): N-sample agreement + semantic\n87\t  entropy → ABSTAIN, on **no-oracle bindings only**; the entropy is a confidence *penalty*, never\n88\t  a boost, and never enters the oracle path.\n89\t- **P6 — value-membership** (`verify/verifier.py`): an out-of-vocabulary `bug_class` is caught at\n90\t  parse time; a hypothesis carries `oracle_provable`.\n91\t- **P7 — conformal coverage** (`calibration/conformal.py`): coverage bands gated on MIN_LABELS,\n92\t  falling back honestly (never a false coverage guarantee).\n93\t\n94\t---\n95\t\n96\t## 4. The learning / metacognition core — N0–N6 (\"nervous system\")\n97\t\n98\tAdditive, default-safe, deterministic, oracle-respecting.\n99\t\n100\t- **Reward + credit** (`calibration/reward_bus.py`, `agents/spine_credit.py`): `outcome_label`\n101\t  single-sources the **non-circular** label (EXPLOITABLE only on ≥2 corroborating oracle kinds).\n102\t  `credit_outcome` fans one outcome to the bandit (check productivity), the calibration ledger\n103\t  (non-circular label), memory priors, and a spine `reward` event. `credit_finding_path` walks\n104\t  the provenance DAG crediting the decisions/hypotheses that led to a confirmed finding.\n105\t- **Multi-critic panel** (`agents/critics.py`): differentiated deterministic critics\n106\t  (grounding / provenance / calibration); `aggregate_panel` — a major objection stands, high\n107\t  disagreement → abstain, else the modal — **never** \"confirm\". `panel_verdict_for` is the quorum\n108\t  gate; `MultiCriticAgent` is addable to the coordinator.\n109\t- **Reflection + cognitive refusal** (`agents/reflection.py`, `agents/cognitive_refusal.py`):\n110\t  `reflect` detects dead threads and stalls and posts re-orienting `reflection` events\n111\t  (re-rank/defer, never skip a surface); `epistemic_refusal` refuses to *conclude* a finding that\n112\t  will not re-ground, recording a `refusal` event.\n113\t- **Governance in the prompt** (`kernel/binding.py`, `framework/cognitive/metacognition.md`): the\n114\t  metacognition/oracle-authority/critic/refusal/self-consistency/learning doctrine is quoted\n115\t  verbatim into **every** LLM system prompt, bounded and cache-stable.\n116\t- **Learning about learning** (`calibration/meta_monitor.py`): `assess_learner_health` reports\n117\t  label count, ECE, Brier, and realized conformal coverage, and recommends *only more caution*\n118\t  (gather evidence / trust confidence less) — never gates. `BanditPolicyProvider` + `rank_by_policy`\n119\t  generalize the bandit's learned value to order (never drop) any decision.\n120\t\n121\tThe RL substrate is the existing Thompson bandit (`scanner/learning.py`) + outcome ledger + PAV\n122\tcalibrator + conformal bands (`calibration/`) + memory priors — the reward bus reuses them; it\n123\tdoes not add a second learner. **The bandit ORDERS effort; it never gates.**\n124\t\n125\t**Wiring status (honest — a primitive is not a live loop).** What is live in the default flagship\n126\t`engage --spine` loop today: the spine mirror of findings/refusals, a per-finding spine `reward`\n127\tevent, and the veracity firewall over findings (P3). What are additive **primitives / schedulable\n128\tagents you opt into** — not run in the default `engage` loop: `credit_outcome` (the full\n129\tbandit+ledger+priors fan-out), `credit_finding_path` (DAG credit), the `MultiCriticAgent` panel,\n130\tthe `ReflectionAgent`, `epistemic_refusal`, and `meta_monitor` are called by their tests and are\n131\taddable to the MAO coordinator / invoked by a caller; they are the building blocks, wired in as\n132\tthe operator adopts them. The doctrine they encode is already live in every reasoning call via\n133\tthe governance preamble (§4, `metacognition.md`).\n134\t\n135\t---\n136\t\n137\t## 5. The safety stack (fail-closed, inviolable)\n138\t\n139\tEvery target-touching action passes, in order: charter/scope (`agents/scope_gate.py`), the\n140\tper-action kill-switch re-read (`authority/killswitch.py`), the egress allowlist, sovereignty\n141\ttier (`kernel/sovereignty.py` — air-gapped fails closed, cloud backends are never even imported\n142\tin strict tiers), and capability entitlement for exploitation (`entitlement/`). These RAISE and\n143\tpropagate — the coordinator re-raises `CrucibleError`; nothing swallows a refusal. Self-\n144\timprovement (`improve/`) is **authorise-not-apply**: it never self-mutates offensive code.\n145\t\n146\t**Declined by design:** capability to evade detection, rotate identities, or stay hidden from\n147\tdefenders. CRUCIBLE is deliberately correlatable and authorized (constitution §VI). The\n148\tconstructive alternative is purple-team detection-efficacy, never anti-defender tradecraft.\n149\t\n150\t---\n151\t\n152\t## 6. Structured workflows (`python3 -m framework.v2 <subcommand>`)\n153\t\n154\t| Goal | Command |\n155\t|------|---------|\n156\t| Authorized end-to-end engagement | `engage <slug> <seed-url> [--recon] [--spine]` |\n157\t| Purple-team defense report (detection gaps + Sigma efficacy + ATT&CK, over offline logs) | `engage … --defender [--defender-sigma DIR] [--defender-log FILE]` |\n158\t| Loopback quick scan (grounded export) | `scan http://127.0.0.1:<port>/ --format json\\|sarif\\|html [--strict-evidence]` |\n159\t| Re-verify a report offline | `verify <report.json>` |\n160\t| Build / verify signed evidence | `evidence …` |\n161\t| OSINT into the shared world-model | `intel …` |\n162\t| Cross-engagement memory / priors | `memory …` |\n163\t| Authorise (not apply) a self-improvement | `improve …` |\n164\t| Read-only operator console (loopback) | `console` |\n165\t| Enumerate the unified capability catalog | `capabilities [--json] [--kind sensor\\|tool\\|oracle\\|operator\\|command]` |\n166\t| Expose gated capabilities as MCP tools (stdio) | `mcp serve --slug <slug>` / `mcp list --slug <slug>` |\n167\t| Status / kill-switch / authority | `status`, `authority …` |\n168\t\n169\tThe **MCP seam** (`mcp/`) is the Wave-6 platformization surface, in two directions, both inside the\n170\tsame safety stack. EXPOSE (`mcp/server.py`): other AI agents/clients enumerate + invoke a *default-safe\n171\tallowlist* of CRUCIBLE capabilities over JSON-RPC-2.0-on-stdio; every `tools/call` routes through the\n172\tSAME `agents.tools.invoke_tool` gate chain, so an unentitled / out-of-scope call is refused over MCP\n173\texactly as locally (the charter `slug` is server-fixed, never request-chosen; nothing exposed is\n174\tungated). CONSUME (`mcp/sensor.py`): an external MCP tool becomes a gated `sensors.base.Sensor` whose\n175\toutput enters the world-model as a provenance-labelled `MCP_TOOL` observation — a **lead, never a\n176\tfact**, until an oracle re-verifies it. All wire input is untrusted (stdlib-only, bounded, safe-parse,\n177\tno eval/shell).\n178\t\n179\tRun any subcommand with `--help` for its flags. Full dispatch table: `framework/v2/__main__.py`.\n180\t\n181\t`capabilities` is a READ-ONLY, deterministic view (`plugins/registry.py::capability_registry`) over CRUCIBLE's\n182\tseveral rosters — the sensor registry, the internal-tool registry, the oracle catalog (`BUG_CLASS_ORACLES`),\n183\tthe operator catalogs, and the CLI table — DISCOVERED, never re-typed. It shows what each capability produces,\n184\tits gating tier / entitlement, and its graceful-absent behaviour. It is the substrate a future MCP/API layer\n185\tenumerates. A plugin publishes a sensor/oracle/operator through `plugins.register_*` (mirroring\n186\t`ToolRegistry.register`) and it appears — building the catalog is metadata only and never runs a capability\n187\t(registration ≠ invocation; every capability stays gated at run time).\n188\t\n189\t---\n190\t\n191\t## 7. When you extend CRUCIBLE\n192\t\n193\t- Keep it **additive + default-safe**: a new sink/flag OFF must leave `engage`/`scan` byte-identical.\n194\t- Preserve **oracle authority**: your addition advises/re-ranks/abstains — it never promotes.\n195\t- Keep it **deterministic**: caller-supplied seq, injected rng; no wallclock/global-rng in\n196\t  learning/reward/spine math (it breaks replay + the calibration audit).\n197\t- **Never over-claim** what the deterministic layer enforces — a doc/field/guarantee that\n198\t  promises more than the code verifies (NL entailment, causal hop-linkage, coverage from sparse\n199\t  data) is itself a hallucination. State the boundary.\n200\t- Any full-set read of the append-only spine must **page** (the default read limit truncates) and\n201\t  fail **closed** on an incomplete read.\n202\t"}]},"uuid":"a1605abc-995e-4e61-9513-eed14a793729","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:35.244Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d6be9790-379d-4e34-97ca-1884ba7ff80e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}
