Default\n17\t``mode=\"observe\"`` is read-only. Nothing here is imported by scan/engage/benchmark/__main__ —\n18\t``make gate`` stays byte-identical.\n19\t\n20\tPublic surface:\n21\t    from framework.v2.aegis import Aegis, Surface, Verdict, ActorRef, LLMInteraction\n22\t    aegis = Aegis.from_config({\"deployment_secret\": \"...\"})\n23\t    v = aegis.observe(surface=Surface.REQUEST, actor=ActorRef(ip=\"203.0.113.7\"),\n24\t                      requested_path=\"/__aegis_hp__/xyz\")\n25\t    with aegis.llm_turn(actor, system_prompt_id=\"sp_v7\") as turn:\n26\t        turn.record_input(user_text); turn.record_output(model_out)\n27\t        verdict = turn.verdict()\n28\t\"\"\"\n29\t\n30\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n31\t\n32\timport json\n33\tfrom contextlib import contextmanager\n34\tfrom pathlib import Path\n35\tfrom typing import Any, Iterator\n36\t\n37\tfrom .actor_graph import ActorGraph\n38\tfrom .guard import LLMGuard\n39\tfrom .models import (\n40\t    ActorRef,\n41\t    AegisConfig,\n42\t    AuthActivity,\n43\t    AuthEvent,\n44\t    BeliefRef,\n45\t    CertRef,\n46\t    LLMInteraction,\n47\t    RefuteChannel,\n48\t    Surface,\n49\t    TelemetryEnvelope,\n50\t    Verdict,\n51\t)\n52\tfrom .pipeline import detect\n53\t\n54\t__all__ = [\n55\t    \"Aegis\",\n56\t    \"Surface\",\n57\t    \"Verdict\",\n58\t    \"ActorRef\",\n59\t    \"LLMInteraction\",\n60\t    \"AuthActivity\",\n61\t    \"AuthEvent\",\n62\t    \"TelemetryEnvelope\",\n63\t    \"AegisConfig\",\n64\t    \"CertRef\",\n65\t    \"BeliefRef\",\n66\t    \"RefuteChannel\",\n67\t    \"LLMGuard\",\n68\t    \"ActorGraph\",\n69\t    \"detect\",\n70\t]\n71\t\n72\t\n73\tclass LLMTurn:\n74\t    \"\"\"A single LLM turn's recorder (tier B). The app records the untrusted input and the\n75\t    model output (and optionally a clean-control-vs-treatment behavior pair), then asks for a\n76\t    verdict. Passive: AEGIS never calls the LLM.\"\"\"\n77\t\n78\t    def __init__(self, aegis: \"Aegis\", actor: ActorRef, *, system_prompt_id: str,\n79\t                 canary: str, seq: int) -> None:\n80\t        self._aegis = aegis\n81\t        self._actor = actor\n82\t        self._system_prompt_id = system_prompt_id\n83\t        self._canary = canary\n84\t        self._seq = seq\n85\t        self._input = \"\"\n86\t        self._output = \"\"\n87\t        self._control: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n88\t        self._treatment: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n89\t\n90\t    def record_input(self, user_text: str) -> None:\n91\t        self._input = user_text\n92\t\n93\t    def record_output(self, output: str) -> None:\n94\t        self._output = output\n95\t\n96\t    def record_behavior(self, *, control: dict[str, Any], treatment: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n97\t        \"\"\"Record a clean-control-turn behavior vs the attacker-treatment-turn behavior — the\n98\t        ONLY path that earns the adversarial ``prompt_injection`` class.\"\"\"\n99\t        self._control = dict(control)\n100\t        self._treatment = dict(treatment)\n101\t\n102\t    def verdict(self) -> Verdict:\n103\t        llm = LLMInteraction(\n104\t            system_prompt_id=self._system_prompt_id, canary=self._canary,\n105\t            user_input=self._input, llm_output=self._output,\n106\t            control_behavior=self._control, treatment_behavior=self._treatment)\n107\t        return self._aegis.observe(surface=Surface.LLM, actor=self._actor, seq=self._seq, llm=llm)\n108\t\n109\t\n110\tclass Aegis:\n111\t    \"\"\"The embeddable facade. Holds the config, the guard (planted canary + honeypot registry),\n112\t    and the continuously-updated per-actor belief graph. Thread-affinity is the caller's\n113\t    concern; scoring is a pure function of the input, so parallel callers with distinct\n114\t    ``seq`` streams stay deterministic.\"\"\"\n115\t\n116\t    def __init__(self, config: AegisConfig, *, guard: LLMGuard | None = None,\n117\t                 actor_graph: ActorGraph | None = None) -> None:\n118\t        self.config = config\n119\t        self.guard = guard if guard is not None else LLMGuard(\n120\t            honeypot_paths=list(config.honeypot_paths) or None,\n121\t            crawler_allowlist=list(config.crawler_allowlist) or None)\n122\t        # keep config.honeypot_paths in sync with the guard so the sensor sees the same set.\n123\t        if not config.honeypot_paths:\n124\t            self.config = config.model_copy(update={\"honeypot_paths\": self.guard.honeypot_paths})\n125\t        self.actor_graph = actor_graph if actor_graph is not None else ActorGraph()\n126\t        self._seq = 0\n127\t\n128\t    # -- construction ------------------------------------------------------\n129\t    @classmethod\n130\t    def from_config(cls, source: \"str | Path | dict[str, Any] | AegisConfig\", *,\n131\t                    guard: LLMGuard | None = None) -> \"Aegis\":\n132\t        \"\"\"Build from a mapping, an ``AegisConfig``, or a path to a JSON/TOML config file.\n133\t        (Loads config, warms the guard/oracles, opens the belief graph.)\"\"\"\n134\t        if isinstance(source, AegisConfig):\n135\t            config = source\n136\t        elif isinstance(source, dict):\n137\t            config = AegisConfig.from_mapping(source)\n138\t        else:\n139\t            config = _load_config_file(Path(source))\n140\t        return cls(config, guard=guard)\n141\t\n142\t    # -- monotonic sequence ------------------------------------------------\n143\t    def next_seq(self) -> int:\n144\t        \"\"\"A process-monotonic sequence for callers that don't supply their own. Monotonic,\n145\t        deterministic per process, never wallclock.\"\"\"\n146\t        self._seq += 1\n147\t        return self._seq\n148\t\n149\t    # -- passive detection (tier A) ---------------------------------------\n150\t    def observe(self, *, surface: Surface, actor: ActorRef, seq: int | None = None,\n151\t                requested_path: str | None = None, llm: LLMInteraction | None = None,\n152\t                auth: AuthActivity | None = None,\n153\t                crawler_allowlisted: bool = False) -> Verdict:\n154\t        \"\"\"Ingest one telemetry unit and return a Verdict. PASSIVE and read-only — the default\n155\t        ``observe`` mode never invokes a response Tool.\"\"\"\n156\t        seq = self.next_seq() if seq is None else seq\n157\t        env = TelemetryEnvelope(surface=surface, actor=actor, seq=seq,\n158\t                                requested_path=requested_path, llm=llm, auth=auth)\n159\t        return detect(env, config=self.config, guard=self.guard,\n160\t                      actor_graph=self.actor_graph, crawler_allowlisted=crawler_allowlisted)\n161\t\n162\t    # -- auth-outcome window (credential-stuffing / ATO) ------------------\n163\t    def observe_auth(self, actor: ActorRef, events: \"list[AuthEvent | dict[str, Any]]\", *,\n164\t                     benign_sources: list[str] | None = None, seq: int | None = None) -> Verdict:\n165\t        \"\"\"Ingest one actor's ORDERED auth-outcome window and return a Verdict. Confirms\n166\t        ``credential_stuffing`` only when a source's UNSEEN-(account, source) SUCCESSES cross the\n167\t        SPRT + Holm family-wise control; a failed-only burst stays a LEAD. PASSIVE / read-only.\"\"\"\n168\t        norm = [e if isinstance(e, AuthEvent) else AuthEvent.model_validate(e) for e in (events or [])]\n169\t        auth = AuthActivity(events=norm, benign_sources=list(benign_sources or []))\n170\t        return self.observe(surface=Surface.AUTH, actor=actor, seq=seq, auth=auth)\n171\t\n172\t    # -- explicit LLM guard (tier B) --------------------------------------\n173\t    @contextmanager\n174\t    def llm_turn(self, actor: ActorRef, *, system_prompt_id: str = \"\",\n175\t                 canary: str | None = None, seq: int | None = None) -> Iterator[LLMTurn]:\n176\t        \"\"\"Wrap one chatbot/agent turn. The app records input/output; ``turn.verdict()`` runs\n177\t        the canary + control-vs-treatment oracles over the retained I/O.\"\"\"\n178\t        seq = self.next_seq() if seq is None else seq\n179\t        yield LLMTurn(self, actor, system_prompt_id=system_prompt_id,\n180\t                      canary=canary or self.guard.canary, seq=seq)\n181\t\n182\t\n183\tdef _load_config_file(path: Path) -> AegisConfig:\n184\t    text = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n185\t    if path.suffix.lower() == \".toml\":\n186\t        try:\n187\t            import tomllib\n188\t        except ModuleNotFoundError as e:   # pragma: no cover - py<3.11\n189\t            raise RuntimeError(\"TOML config requires Python 3.11+ (tomllib)\") from e\n190\t        data = tomllib.loads(text)\n191\t    else:\n192\t        data = json.loads(text)\n193\t    return AegisConfig.from_mapping(data)\n194\t"}]},"uuid":"b179ab20-1746-4a59-8f0c-c6b7da01f340","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:46.452Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8e76a3a9-6046-442c-87f8-96597f7ae7e4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

Here is the architecture output.\n2\t\n3\t---\n4\t\n5\t# AEGIS ⟵ CRUCIBLE: Concrete Crown-Jewel Reuse Map\n6\t\n7\tEvery claim below cites a real file under `/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main/framework/v2`. The governing principle: **AEGIS is the same prove-don't-guess reasoning core pointed inward at the operator's own app.** A detection is a provenance-tagged *signal*; it is a *confirmed AI attack* only when a deterministic oracle re-fires over retained, re-runnable evidence — the identical contract `verify/reverify.py` already enforces for offensive findings.\n8\t\n9\t---\n10\t\n11\t## 1. The prove-don't-guess oracle layer — REUSE THE PATTERN, ADD KINDS\n12\t\n13\t**Files:** `verify/oracles.py`, `verify/verifier.py`, `verify/models.py`, `verify/adapter.py`, `verify/reverify.py`\n14\t\n15\t**What is reused verbatim:**\n16\t- **`OracleSignal` / `VerificationResult`** (`verify/models.py:86,110`) and the **`OracleVerifier.confirm`** dispatch (`verify/verifier.py:237`) — the \"no oracle, no confirmation; an absent input yields a non-firing signal, never an assumed pass\" gate. AEGIS confirmations flow through the *same* verifier.\n17\t- **`FindingContext` + `to_verifier_context()`** (`verify/adapter.py:122,461`) — the typed, JSON-serialisable carrier of *already-observed* evidence. This is exactly what an AEGIS detection needs: the SDK collects request/LLM telemetry, packs it into a `FindingContext`, and the oracle judges it. The adapter is explicitly a *translator, not a generator* (`adapter.py:10`) — it never touches the target — which matches AEGIS's \"ingest telemetry, never attack\" doctrine.\n18\t- **`reverify_context` / `reverify_finding`** (`verify/reverify.py:95,187`) — every AEGIS detection ships a certificate that anyone can re-run offline with no app and no trust in AEGIS. The memoization cache just added on this branch (`reverify.py:77`, `_REVERIFY_CACHE_MAX`) applies unchanged.\n19\t- **Several existing oracles fire on AEGIS inputs with zero modification:**\n20\t  - `boolean_inference_oracle` (SPRT, `oracles.py:471`), `timing_oracle` (Mann-Whitney U + Hodges-Lehmann + dose-response, `oracles.py:363`), and `holm_correction` (`oracles.py:548`) are *exactly* the statistics needed to separate **machine-speed adaptive attacks (AI credential-stuffing, agentic probing) from jitter/noise** across many requests. This is the single biggest \"already built\" win — the hard math for behavioral/timing bot detection is done.\n21\t  - `reflection_context_oracle` (`oracles.py:781`) and `evaluation_oracle` (`oracles.py:840`) already prove \"an injected string reached an *executable* position / was *evaluated* rather than merely reflected.\" That control-vs-treatment discipline is the template for the new prompt-injection oracle (see §9).\n22\t\n23\t**The seam AEGIS plugs into:** the `BUG_CLASS_ORACLES` table + `known_bug_classes()` value-membership (`verify/verifier.py:33,186`). AEGIS registers **new `OracleKind` members** (`verify/models.py:31`) and **new `bug_class → oracle` rows**, and — critically — extends `known_bug_classes()` so an AI-attack class asserted as oracle-provable can't be a hallucinated label. `require_known_bug_class` (`verifier.py:205`) already rejects out-of-vocabulary classes at pydantic parse time.\n24\t\n25\t**NEW:** the AI-attack `OracleKind` values, the `BUG_CLASS_ORACLES` entries, the new `FindingContext` fields/builders, and the new oracle *bodies* (§9).\n26\t\n27\t---\n28\t\n29\t## 2. The unified world-model — REUSE VERBATIM (beliefs + grounding tiers)\n30\t\n31\t**File:** `worldmodel/models.py`\n32\t\n33\t- **Beta-belief accumulation** (`Node.alpha/beta`, `belief_mean`, `belief_lcb`, `models.py:203-236`) is how AEGIS accumulates evidence about a *suspected AI actor* (an IP, session, or identity) across many requests: each corroborating signal raises `belief_mean`; a refuting observation (a provably-human interaction) lowers it — the thing a scalar \"risk score\" structurally cannot express. `belief_lcb` gives the honest \"thinly-evidenced actor scores below a proven one.\"\n34\t- **Provenance grounding tiers** (`classify_provenance`, `GROUNDING_INTEL` vs `GROUNDING_GROUNDED`, `models.py:159-181`) *are* AEGIS's signal→confirmed pipeline: a detection enters as `intel:` (real, not proof), and only a fired oracle / signed cert promotes it to `grounded:`. This is the single source of truth the veracity firewall reuses.\n35\t- Existing kinds that map directly: `SESSION`, `PRINCIPAL`, `IDENTITY` (OSINT persona), `INDICATOR` (IOC), `CONTROL` (a defensive control), and edges `REACHED`, `AUTHENTICATES_TO`, `CONTROL_PROTECTS`, `OBSERVED_ON` (`models.py:48-143`).\n36\t\n37\t**NEW:** a small set of node/edge kinds for the *external-actor* view — e.g. an `ACTOR`/attacker-session node and `ISSUED`/`TARGETS`/`EXHIBITS` edges (attacker → request → app-surface). Some can reuse `SESSION`+`INDICATOR`; a couple are genuinely new enum members. Also NEW: a **windowing/eviction policy** for a *continuously-running* actor graph (CRUCIBLE's graph is engagement-batch; AEGIS is a live stream).\n38\t\n39\t---\n40\t\n41\t## 3. The veracity firewall — REUSE VERBATIM (the only-demotes choke point)\n42\t\n43\t**File:** `veracity/firewall.py` — `admit()` (`firewall.py:105`)\n44\t\n45\tThis is AEGIS's anti-hallucination guarantee, for free. Every \"this was an AI attack\" claim is constructed as a `Claim` and passed through `admit()`, which **re-executes** each cited ground *bound to the claim's own bug_class* (`_oracle_ok` calls `reverify_context`, `firewall.py:50-63`) and **can only demote or abstain** (`firewall.py:15-19`). A fabricated actor not in the world-model → `UNGROUNDED` (`firewall.py:123`); a dry-run/LLM-only detection → cannot reach fact strength (`firewall.py:154`). AEGIS reuses this unchanged; it only builds `Claim`s.\n46\t\n47\t**NEW:** nothing in the firewall. AEGIS supplies `Claim`/`GroundingToken` objects (existing `veracity/claims.py`, `veracity/tokens.py`).\n48\t\n49\t---\n50\t\n51\t## 4. Intel Observation + projection + the Sensor framework — REUSE VERBATIM (universal producer)\n52\t\n53\t**Files:** `sensors/base.py`, `intel/models.py`, `intel/project.py`\n54\t\n55\tThe **Sensor protocol** (`sensors/base.py:37`) *is* the AEGIS ingestion abstraction. `sensors/base.py:1-21` states it: a Sensor is a gated `Tool` plus a `normalize(result) → list[Observation]` step, minting **observations, never facts**, entering the graph as `GROUNDING_INTEL` and becoming a FACT only if an oracle re-verifies (`base.py:13-16`). That is a verbatim statement of AEGIS doctrine.\n56\t\n57\t- `Observation` (`intel/models.py:90`) with Admiralty source-reliability, `polarity` (a *refuting* observation — a provably-human signal — drives belief DOWN, `models.py:118`), and monotonic `seq` (no wallclock).\n58\t- `project_observation` (`intel/project.py:35`) — THE KEYSTONE: streaming Path A projects telemetry onto the world-model and gets corroboration/refutation/provenance for free via the Beta upsert. Order-independent and deterministic (`project.py:35-43`).\n59\t- `observation_to_evidence` (`intel/project.py:85`) bridges an Observation into a `confidence.Evidence` for the SCE (§6).\n60\t\n61\t**NEW:** concrete AEGIS sensors implementing the protocol — `RequestTelemetrySensor`, `LLMInteractionSensor`, `ContentSubmissionSensor` — plus new `IntelSourceKind` members (`intel/models.py:39`: add `request_telemetry`, `llm_interaction`, `content_submission`). The **shared minter pattern** in `service_observations` (`sensors/base.py:67`) is the template for a shared actor/request minter.\n62\t\n63\t---\n64\t\n65\t## 5. The signed event spine — REUSE VERBATIM (tamper-evident audit of every determination)\n66\t\n67\t**Files:** `agents/blackboard.py`, `agents/spine_chain.py`, `agents/models.py`, `agents/spine_sink.py`\n68\t\n69\tAEGIS emits every detection-lifecycle event onto the append-only, hash-linked, governance-signed spine — exactly what a defensive product needs for a defensible audit trail. The existing `EventKind` vocabulary already covers AEGIS's needs (`agents/models.py:29-46`): `tool_call`/`tool_result` (a gated sensor ran — \"a PROVENANCE-labelled observation, not a fact\"), `finding` (a confirmed detection), `refusal` (a gate fired — \"refusals are evidence\"), `reward` (a learning signal), `critic_verdict`, `reflection`. `spine_chain.py` gives content+order tamper-evidence with the \"no wallclock in digests\" discipline (`spine_chain.py:1-19`), reused verbatim.\n70\t\n71\t**NEW:** minimal. AEGIS writes through the duck-typed `SpineSink`. The one genuinely new concern is **spine volume/retention under continuous production** (CRUCIBLE assumes bounded engagements) — the X3 paging/cursor work in `blackboard.replay` (`spine_chain.py:_events` pages to exhaustion) mitigates but a retention/rollup policy is new.\n72\t\n73\t---\n74\t\n75\t## 6. Scientific Confidence Engine — REUSE (calibrated posterior with competing benign explanations)\n76\t\n77\t**File:** `confidence/decision.py` — `assess_finding` (`decision.py:63`)\n78\t\n79\tThis is how AEGIS avoids false positives: a confirmed detection is weighed against **the specific benign explanation that masquerades as it** — a fast human vs a bot, a legit edge-case prompt vs a jailbreak — as a real MECE competitor (`decision.py:44-54,92-107`). The oracle stays the authority; SCE expresses *how confident* (`decision.py:12-16`). Output: \"posterior 0.994, top alternative 0.004.\"\n80\t\n81\t**NEW:** entries in `_ALTERNATIVES` and `_CONFIRMATION_LR` (`decision.py:33-53`) for AI-attack classes (e.g. `behavioral_evasion_bot → (\"fast-human\", …)`, `prompt_injection → (\"benign-edge-prompt\", …)`).\n82\t\n83\t---\n84\t\n85\t## 7. Calibration / reward bus — REUSE VERBATIM (AEGIS learns which detectors are productive)\n86\t\n87\t**File:** `calibration/reward_bus.py` — `credit_outcome` (`reward_bus.py:52`)\n88\t\n89\tWhen an operator confirms or dismisses a detection (the ground-truth label), `credit_outcome` fans that outcome to the bandit (orders detector effort), the calibration ledger (keeps confidence honest over time), and cross-engagement priors — best-effort, each sink independent (`reward_bus.py:72-80`). This is AEGIS's online learning loop, reused as-is; it emits a `reward` event to the same spine sink.\n90\t\n91\t**NEW:** nothing structural — AEGIS supplies the `bug_class`/`surface_pattern`/`spine_sink` arguments and an operator feedback channel.\n92\t\n93\t---\n94\t\n95\t## 8. Fail-closed gate chain — REUSE VERBATIM (keeps AEGIS default-safe & non-offensive)\n96\t\n97\t**Files:** `agents/tools/invoker.py`, `agents/tools/base.py`, `authority/`, `entitlement/`, `agents/scope_gate.py`, `agents/egress_guard.py`\n98\t\n99\tAEGIS's **active responses** (challenge, throttle, alert, block) are the only part that acts, and each is a `Tool` run through `invoke_tool` (`invoker.py:143`): kill-switch → entitlement → scope → destructive-confirm → egress (`invoker.py:80-140`), every gate fail-closed, every call recorded on the spine before it runs (`invoker.py:160-171`). This is what makes AEGIS **DEFAULT-SAFE and gated**: passive detection is read-only and off this path; any response is opt-in, entitlement-gated, and a tripped kill-switch halts all AEGIS response. Its \"correlatable, not anti-defender\" posture is inherited by construction.\n100\t\n101\t**NEW:** the response-action `Tool` implementations themselves (they *ride* the existing gate; they don't modify it).\n102\t\n103\t---\n104\t\n105\t## 9. What is genuinely NEW (must be built)\n106\t\n107\t**A. New oracle bodies** (in an `aegis/oracles.py`, same purity contract as `verify/oracles.py`):\n108\t1. **`prompt_injection_oracle`** — over retained LLM prompt/response/control triples, fire only when an injected directive *provably altered* the model's output vs a control that omits it. Structurally the `evaluation_oracle` pattern (`oracles.py:840`): effect present + control clean + not merely reflected. Covers direct *and* indirect (content-borne) injection against the app's own chatbot/RAG.\n109\t2. **`behavioral_bot_oracle`** — over retained inter-request timing / navigation traces, reuse the **existing** `_mann_whitney`/`_hodges_lehmann`/SPRT machinery (`oracles.py:328-545`) to fire on machine-generated regularity beyond a floor. Mostly assembly of existing statistics.\n110\t3. **`credential_stuffing_oracle`** — over retained auth-attempt telemetry, deterministic signature of distributed low-per-identity machine-speed attempts; reuse `holm_correction` (`oracles.py:548`) for the multi-identity family-wise control.\n111\t4. **Adversarial-ML oracles** (`model_extraction`, `membership_inference`, `synthetic_content`) — genuinely new deterministic checks over retained query distributions; no existing analogue.\n112\t\n113\t**B. New vocabulary/models** (extensions, not rewrites):\n114\t- New `OracleKind` members (`verify/models.py:31`) + `BUG_CLASS_ORACLES` rows + `known_bug_classes()` entries (`verify/verifier.py`).\n115\t- New `FindingContext` fields + builders (`verify/adapter.py`) for LLM I/O, timing traces, auth telemetry.\n116\t- New `IntelSourceKind` members (`intel/models.py:39`); a few new `NodeKind`/`EdgeKind` for the external-actor view (`worldmodel/models.py`).\n117\t- New `_ALTERNATIVES`/`_CONFIRMATION_LR` rows (`confidence/decision.py`).\n118\t\n119\t**C. New infrastructure** (no CRUCIBLE analogue):\n120\t- **The embeddable API/SDK surface** — an *inbound* ingestion endpoint + client library a third-party app integrates. CRUCIBLE has no inbound API; this is the core new artifact. Must enforce **bounded sizes + safe parsing (no eval/shell) + PII redaction** at the boundary (partially reuses X2 at-rest redaction, but the untrusted-input parse layer is new).\n121\t- **The `framework/v2/aegis/` package** — additive, OFF the default scan/engage/gate path so `make gate` stays byte-identical (the doctrine every prior wave held).\n122\t- **A continuous online detection loop** with actor-graph windowing/eviction and spine retention/rollup (CRUCIBLE is batch-per-engagement).\n123\t- **Response-action `Tool`s** (challenge/throttle/block) registered in a `ToolRegistry`.\n124\t\n125\t---\n126\t\n127\t## One-line summary\n128\t\n129\tAEGIS builds **~4 new oracle bodies + one inbound API/SDK + a continuous loop**, and reuses everything load-bearing unchanged: the **verifier/adapter/reverify certificate pipeline** (`verify/`), the **veracity `admit()` only-demotes firewall** (`veracity/firewall.py`), the **Sensor→Observation→`project_observation` producer keystone** (`sensors/base.py`,`intel/project.py`) with **Beta-belief actor scoring** (`worldmodel/models.py`), the **signed event spine** (`agents/blackboard.py`,`spine_chain.py`), the **SCE calibrated-posterior gate** (`confidence/decision.py`), the **reward/calibration loop** (`calibration/reward_bus.py`), and the **fail-closed `invoke_tool` gate chain** (`agents/tools/invoker.py`) that keeps every AEGIS response opt-in, gated, and non-offensive."}]},"uuid":"c448f591-4d00-4a6d-9161-90602acf3eb1","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:47.181Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"be2b5342-1149-4014-8bfb-1106f46eedf0","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"ba16e7a1-2134-4f54-9e9a-4a567c576d24","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d1e39661-f405-4984-bff2-6dce9f596a9d","agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Kee1xysioXxcLY4KbWKAAB","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"\n2\tentitlement.crypto — Ed25519 primitives and m-of-n threshold verify.\n3\t\n4\tWe do not roll our own crypto. Ed25519 sign/verify is `cryptography`\n5\t(pyca); we wrap it for typed errors and a clean threshold API.\n6\t\n7\tThreshold model: an entitlement is valid iff at least `trust_root.threshold`\n8\t*distinct* authorisers from the trust root produced a valid signature\n9\tover the canonical bytes. Properties enforced here:\n10\t\n11\t  - A signature whose key_id is not in the trust root does not count.\n12\t  - A key_id cannot be counted twice (duplicate signatures collapse).\n13\t  - An invalid signature does not count and does not abort the tally —\n14\t    we count valid distinct contributors and compare to the threshold.\n15\t\n16\tThis is a simple, fully-verifiable multisig. It is forward-compatible\n17\twith FROST-Ed25519: a FROST group produces a single standard Ed25519\n18\tsignature verifiable against the group public key, so a trust root with\n19\tone authoriser (the group key) and threshold 1 verifies a FROST\n20\tsignature with no change to this code.\n21\t\n22\tSigning helpers (`generate_keypair`, `sign`) exist for operator-side\n23\tprovisioning tooling and tests. The runtime only ever *verifies*.\n24\t\"\"\"\n25\t\n26\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n27\t\n28\timport base64\n29\timport binascii\n30\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n31\t\n32\tfrom cryptography.exceptions import InvalidSignature\n33\tfrom cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.ed25519 import (\n34\t    Ed25519PrivateKey,\n35\t    Ed25519PublicKey,\n36\t)\n37\tfrom cryptography.hazmat.primitives.serialization import (\n38\t    Encoding,\n39\t    NoEncryption,\n40\t    PrivateFormat,\n41\t    PublicFormat,\n42\t)\n43\t\n44\tfrom ..common.errors import EntitlementError\n45\tfrom .models import AuthorizerKey, Signature, TrustRoot\n46\t\n47\t\n48\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n49\t# Key encoding\n50\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n51\t\n52\t\n53\tdef _b64decode_exact(value: str, expected_len: int, what: str) -> bytes:\n54\t    try:\n55\t        raw = base64.b64decode(value, validate=True)\n56\t    except (ValueError, binascii.Error) as e:\n57\t        raise EntitlementError(f\"{what} is not valid base64: {e}\") from e\n58\t    if len(raw) != expected_len:\n59\t        raise EntitlementError(\n60\t            f\"{what} decodes to {len(raw)} bytes, expected {expected_len}\"\n61\t        )\n62\t    return raw\n63\t\n64\t\n65\tdef load_public_key(public_key_b64: str) -> Ed25519PublicKey:\n66\t    raw = _b64decode_exact(public_key_b64, 32, \"Ed25519 public key\")\n67\t    return Ed25519PublicKey.from_public_bytes(raw)\n68\t\n69\t\n70\tdef verify_one(public_key_b64: str, message: bytes, signature_b64: str) -> bool:\n71\t    \"\"\"True iff `signature_b64` is a valid Ed25519 signature over\n72\t    `message` under `public_key_b64`. Never raises on a bad signature —\n73\t    returns False — but raises EntitlementError on malformed key/sig\n74\t    material (a provisioning error, not an attacker-controlled path).\"\"\"\n75\t    pub = load_public_key(public_key_b64)\n76\t    sig = _b64decode_exact(signature_b64, 64, \"Ed25519 signature\")\n77\t    try:\n78\t        pub.verify(sig, message)\n79\t        return True\n80\t    except InvalidSignature:\n81\t        return False\n82\t\n83\t\n84\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n85\t# Threshold verification\n86\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n87\t\n88\t\n89\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n90\tclass ThresholdResult:\n91\t    \"\"\"Outcome of a threshold check over a canonical message.\"\"\"\n92\t\n93\t    satisfied: bool\n94\t    valid_signers: tuple[str, ...]   # distinct key_ids that signed validly\n95\t    threshold: int\n96\t    reason: str\n97\t\n98\t\n99\tdef verify_threshold(\n100\t    message: bytes,\n101\t    signatures: list[Signature],\n102\t    trust_root: TrustRoot,\n103\t) -> ThresholdResult:\n104\t    \"\"\"Count distinct trust-root authorisers with a valid signature over\n105\t    `message`; compare to the threshold.\"\"\"\n106\t    by_id: dict[str, AuthorizerKey] = {a.key_id: a for a in trust_root.authorizers}\n107\t\n108\t    valid: list[str] = []\n109\t    seen: set[str] = set()\n110\t    for sig in signatures:\n111\t        if sig.key_id in seen:\n112\t            continue  # a key contributes at most once\n113\t        seen.add(sig.key_id)\n114\t        authorizer = by_id.get(sig.key_id)\n115\t        if authorizer is None:\n116\t            continue  # not a trusted authoriser\n117\t        if verify_one(authorizer.public_key_b64, message, sig.signature_b64):\n118\t            valid.append(sig.key_id)\n119\t\n120\t    satisfied = len(valid) >= trust_root.threshold\n121\t    if satisfied:\n122\t        reason = (\n123\t            f\"{len(valid)} valid distinct signature(s) \"\n124\t            f\">= threshold {trust_root.threshold}\"\n125\t        )\n126\t    else:\n127\t        reason = (\n128\t            f\"only {len(valid)} valid distinct signature(s) \"\n129\t            f\"< threshold {trust_root.threshold} \"\n130\t            f\"(trust root has {len(trust_root.authorizers)} authoriser(s))\"\n131\t        )\n132\t    return ThresholdResult(\n133\t        satisfied=satisfied,\n134\t        valid_signers=tuple(valid),\n135\t        threshold=trust_root.threshold,\n136\t        reason=reason,\n137\t    )\n138\t\n139\t\n140\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n141\t# Provisioning helpers (operator-side issuance + tests; never the runtime)\n142\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n143\t\n144\t\n145\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n146\tclass KeyPair:\n147\t    \"\"\"A freshly generated Ed25519 keypair, base64-encoded. The private\n148\t    key is the issuance secret; it never touches the runtime host.\"\"\"\n149\t\n150\t    public_key_b64: str\n151\t    private_key_b64: str\n152\t\n153\t\n154\tdef generate_keypair() -> KeyPair:\n155\t    priv = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()\n156\t    raw_priv = priv.private_bytes(Encoding.Raw, PrivateFormat.Raw, NoEncryption())\n157\t    raw_pub = priv.public_key().public_bytes(Encoding.Raw, PublicFormat.Raw)\n158\t    return KeyPair(\n159\t        public_key_b64=base64.b64encode(raw_pub).decode(\"ascii\"),\n160\t        private_key_b64=base64.b64encode(raw_priv).decode(\"ascii\"),\n161\t    )\n162\t\n163\t\n164\tdef sign(private_key_b64: str, message: bytes) -> str:\n165\t    \"\"\"Produce a base64 Ed25519 signature. Issuance tooling / tests only.\"\"\"\n166\t    raw = _b64decode_exact(private_key_b64, 32, \"Ed25519 private key\")\n167\t    priv = Ed25519PrivateKey.from_private_bytes(raw)\n168\t    return base64.b64encode(priv.sign(message)).decode(\"ascii\")\n169\t"}]},"uuid":"8b56a00e-39ee-4ec5-884d-2c3a130a74d4","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:56.822Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ba16e7a1-2134-4f54-9e9a-4a567c576d24","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

{"parentUuid":"d5b0489c-f1d4-4700-9929-5b97f77bd4bc","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d1e39661-f405-4984-bff2-6dce9f596a9d","agentId":"acfd66bc128b0f7d7","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JAaFAc37cTq3XyiNee9Rtc","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"\n2\tauthority.charter — derive an engagement authority from the charter.\n3\t\n4\tThe charter is already the binding authorization document (signed,\n5\tscoped). This builds an EngagementAuthority from it so an engagement\n6\tcarries a scoped, time-boxed authority by default rather than the\n7\toperator hand-writing one. `authority_from_scope` is the pure core;\n8\t`authority_from_charter` reads the charter's in-scope host table via the\n9\texisting ethics parser.\n10\t\n11\tDefaults are conservative: destructive actions OFF, a bounded validity\n12\twindow, and a finite action budget. The operator widens these\n13\tdeliberately (and, at high assurance, threshold-signs the result).\n14\t\"\"\"\n15\t\n16\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n17\t\n18\tfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone\n19\t\n20\tfrom ..common import ethics\n21\tfrom ..common.errors import OutOfScope\n22\tfrom .models import EngagementAuthority, TargetEnvironment\n23\t\n24\t\n25\tdef authority_from_scope(\n26\t    slug: str,\n27\t    scope: list[str],\n28\t    *,\n29\t    environment: TargetEnvironment = TargetEnvironment.TWIN,\n30\t    duration_hours: float = 8.0,\n31\t    allow_destructive: bool = False,\n32\t    max_actions: int = 1000,\n33\t    issued_by: str = \"\",\n34\t    now: datetime | None = None,\n35\t) -> EngagementAuthority:\n36\t    \"\"\"Build an authority from an explicit scope list. Fail closed on an\n37\t    empty scope — an authority that authorises nothing is not useful, and\n38\t    an empty scope usually means a parse problem upstream.\"\"\"\n39\t    if not scope:\n40\t        raise OutOfScope(\n41\t            f\"cannot build an engagement authority for {slug!r}: empty scope\"\n42\t        )\n43\t    ts = now or datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n44\t    return EngagementAuthority(\n45\t        engagement_slug=slug,\n46\t        environment=environment,\n47\t        scope=scope,\n48\t        not_before=ts,\n49\t        not_after=ts + timedelta(hours=duration_hours),\n50\t        allow_destructive=allow_destructive,\n51\t        max_actions=max_actions,\n52\t        issued_by=issued_by,\n53\t    )\n54\t\n55\t\n56\tdef authority_from_charter(\n57\t    slug: str,\n58\t    *,\n59\t    environment: TargetEnvironment = TargetEnvironment.TWIN,\n60\t    duration_hours: float = 8.0,\n61\t    allow_destructive: bool = False,\n62\t    max_actions: int = 1000,\n63\t    issued_by: str = \"\",\n64\t    now: datetime | None = None,\n65\t) -> EngagementAuthority:\n66\t    \"\"\"Build an authority from the charter's in-scope host table. Raises\n67\t    OutOfScope if the charter declares no in-scope hosts.\"\"\"\n68\t    scope = ethics.parse_scope(slug)\n69\t    return authority_from_scope(\n70\t        slug, scope,\n71\t        environment=environment, duration_hours=duration_hours,\n72\t        allow_destructive=allow_destructive, max_actions=max_actions,\n73\t        issued_by=issued_by, now=now,\n74\t    )\n75\t"}]},"uuid":"f14823ab-a004-4ae1-be93-9af23f9c1dbe","timestamp":"2026-07-20T01:10:57.262Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d5b0489c-f1d4-4700-9929-5b97f77bd4bc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-vscode","cwd":"/home/kali/Pictures/PENTEST-main","sessionId":"7758e121-f349-47d5-886b-6bb5a1d60e27","version":"2.1.214","gitBranch":"main","slug":"parsed-popping-lemur"}

`sha256_hex`, `digest_payload`, `evidence_signing_bytes`.\n- **Ed25519 crypto + threshold** — `framework/v2/entitlement/crypto.py`: pyca `cryptography`; `verify_one`, `verify_threshold` (m-of-n distinct authorisers), `generate_keypair`/`sign` (provisioning only). \"We do not roll our own crypto.\"\n- **Event spine / blackboard** — `framework/v2/agents/blackboard.py` (append-only SQLite; only `post()`/`supersede()`; SQL triggers refuse UPDATE/DELETE) and `framework/v2/agents/spine_chain.py` (hash-links events via the evidence primitives, governance-signed head, **fails closed** on a truncated read, binds head to engagement slug).\n\n**Divergence — this matters for the merge.** SIGIL's `sigil/reuse/chain.py` is **AHEAD of** CRUCIBLE's `evidence/chain.py`, not a stale copy:\n- SIGIL added a **v2 head schema** (fields `base_seq, base_prev_hash, base_count, cumulative_merkle_root, snapshot_seq, prev_head_hash`) supporting a **re-based / pruned live window with a cumulative Merkle root** — i.e. log rollup/retention for continuous operation. `verify_chain(entries, *, genesis_prev=…)` and `verify_head(…, genesis_prev=…)` take the rebase point; `entry_count` becomes absolute (`base_count + len(entries)`).\n- Backward-compatibility is deliberate: a `schema_version < 2` head drops the v2 fields ENTIRELY from the signing payload (explicit pop, not `exclude_unset`), so v1 heads verify byte-identically. CRUCIBLE's copy is still **v1** (no pruning/Merkle, no `genesis_prev` param).\n- `canonical.py`: behaviourally **byte-identical** (same domain tag, same functions) — only docstrings/comments differ.\n- `crypto.py`: sole change is a local `IntegrityError` in place of CRUCIBLE's `EntitlementError` (decoupling).\n- `models.py`: SIGIL consolidated the pydantic classes from CRUCIBLE's two `models.py` into one.\n\nNet: SIGIL forked the integrity substrate and evolved it (continuous-stream Merkle pruning) while remaining wire-compatible with CRUCIBLE's v1 heads. A monorepo merge would need to decide whether CRUCIBLE adopts SIGIL's v2 chain as the shared source of truth, or the two keep separate copies.\n\n## 3.
